# Project Nyx — Backlog

A running list of items intentionally deferred from the current build sequence. This is the destination for anything that would otherwise be said as "let's log that for the future."

**Access:** PM can type `view backlog`, `show backlog`, or any natural-language equivalent in a session — Claude reads this file and presents it grouped by priority, surfacing anything that blocks the current build phase first.

**Adding items:** Claude adds rows here proactively — do not wait for PM. When raising a "for the future" item mid-session, write it here before continuing. Include a one-line _why_ so future-you can re-evaluate without re-deriving the context.

**IDs:** sequential `B-NNN`. Never reuse. When closing an item, leave the row and mark `Status: Done — <session date>` rather than deleting.

**Priority:**
- **Now** — should be tackled in the current or next session; here only because it's outside the in-flight chunk
- **Next** — tackle within the next 2–3 sessions or the next build phase
- **Later** — real but not time-sensitive; revisit when conditions change (e.g. pre-prod, multi-pet, paid tier)

**Status — lead with a structured head.** The Status cell must **begin** with one state token from this fixed set, then the date and resolving PR, so the column reads true at a glance and drift is machine-checkable:

- `Open` — not started.
- `In progress — <date> (PR #N)` — actively being built; say what's left.
- `Partial — <date> (PR #N)` — a defined slice shipped, more deferred; name the remaining slices.
- `Blocked — <reason>` — can't proceed; state the blocker (usually an Open Question).
- `Done — <date> (PR #N)` — shipped. Keep the row (don't delete); never close without a resolving PR/session reference.

**Rewrite the head when state changes — do NOT append the update to the tail of an already-long cell.** The recurring drift this file suffers is exactly that: a row that *opens* with `draft PR #106` / `R1 slice scoped` while a `Done` marker sits buried 200 words later — so the column lies even though the truth is technically "in the row" (caught reactively as B-022/B-045 on 2026-05-31, B-040/B-051/B-075 on 2026-06-11). Narrative and history may stay **below** the head, but the first token must be current. `/wrap` Step 4 reconciles these rows for every B-ID a session touches; the `backlog-groomer` skill enforces the head.

| B-325 | Retroactive + med-first combo linking (the paths B-156 B2b left uncovered) | PM-flagged UX gap (2026-07-12): the med↔food combo link (`paired_event_id`) can ONLY be created on the FORWARD, in-the-moment path — the "+ Add a med given with this" line on the meal/treat completion card (`MealCompletionCard`), which auto-dismisses in ~5s. Code-confirmed: (a) an already-logged treat/meal has NO "add a med" affordance on its detail screen — the `ComboLinkRow` in `event/[id].tsx` + the History `ComboCrossLink` are READ-ONLY, rendering only a link that already exists; (b) the med side has NO combo entry at all — `MedicationCompletionCard` offers only adherence + the descriptive `how_given` (in_treat/in_food) chip, and `edit-event.tsx` has zero paired handling. So both owner-hypotheses hold: you can't add a med to an existing treat, and you can't attach a treat to an existing med. This reopens B-156's own false-adherence hole for the exact target user (Sam pilling a squirming cat misses the 5s window → the intake→adherence safety coupling from PR B3 never runs). Directions differ in weight: **med→EXISTING treat** is a clean reuse of `insertMedicationDose` + `pairedEventId` + the `getMealForEvent` vehicle-intake read (high value, cheap, safety-coupling reuses the forward path); **minting a NEW treat from the med side** forces the pure-vehicle-inflation question (investigation §8 / B-111) that the forward path sidesteps by construction (`how_given` already serves the label-only med-first case). Recommended shape: a retroactive "+ Add a med given with this" on the treat/meal DETAIL screen (a better home than the narrow card — §4) + a med-detail "link to an existing treat"; run the same B3 coupling on both; never mint a phantom vehicle event. | Next | 2026-07-12 | Rides the B-156 promotion decision; extends B-156 (combo); composes with B-176 (Today parity) / B-173 (ate-around-the-pill) | **Partial — direction ① shipped via #342 (2026-07-12); direction ② (med-first) still HELD (B-111).** PM-greenlit & designed via mockup rounds this session. The retroactive "+ Add a med given with this" entry now lives on the meal/treat detail screen (`app/event/[id].tsx`, gated on `food_type ∈ {meal,treat}`), routing to the same pre-bound picker (`comboSource:'detail'`) → linked dose via the existing `insertMedicationDose(…, pairedEventId)` + PR B3 coupling. Design (PM steer + Designer/persona call): no completion-card replay — returns to the treat; a **not-finished** vehicle gets a deliberate **confirm sheet** (`components/log/ComboDoseConfirmSheet.tsx`, heads-up + real adherence chips + "Not sure yet") so the owner inputs how much got in rather than landing silently unconfirmed; unconfirmed survives only as the explicit-skip fallback; adherence stays editable on the dose screen (G2). New `comboConfirmHeadsUp` copy helper (+tests). `adversarial-reviewer` PASS (never a false 'given' across backdrop/skip/write-fail/read-fail; forward path byte-for-byte unchanged); `code-reviewer` clean after token fix. | B-036 | Pinch-to-zoom in the fullscreen PhotoViewer | **Rationale FALSIFIED by the PM 2026-07-26 — the original "low priority" line named only the ingredients case and missed the wedge case entirely.** PM scenario: *in the exam room*, pulling up a vomit photo so the owner and vet can inspect it together. That is the reactive-owner wedge at its highest-stakes moment, and "ingredient text is already captured by extraction" says nothing about it (nor about a failed/low-confidence extraction, where the photo IS the record — see the `ingredient list not read` D10 gate). Scope note from the 2026-07-26 discovery: `components/ui/PhotoViewer.tsx` is the single lightbox behind **4 of 5** in-app photo surfaces (event detail, edit-event, medication detail, food carousel) — so the gesture work is one component, not an app-wide sweep. Two hard constraints found: **(1)** neither `react-native-gesture-handler` nor `react-native-reanimated` is installed (both pinned in SDK 57: `~2.32.0` / `4.5.0` + `react-native-worklets@0.10.0`; New Arch on by default, so Reanimated 4 is clean) — a native module means a **native `eas build`, never an OTA**, so this should ride the already-mandatory W6/rebrand TestFlight cut or it costs a build cycle of its own; **(2)** RN's built-in `ScrollView` zoom props (`maximumZoomScale`/`minimumZoomScale`/`pinchGestureEnabled`) are **iOS-only** (`ScrollViewPropsIOS`), which is what makes a zero-dependency iOS-only path possible at all. **Resolution ceiling is the real product limit, and it is independent of the gesture:** `lib/storage.ts` caps uploads at `MAX_EDGE_PX = 1600` / JPEG q75, and `EVENT_PHOTO_TRANSFORM` serves at the same 1600 — so on a 3× phone a fit-to-screen photo is already near 1:1 and zoom buys roughly 1.3–1.4× of *real* detail before it is just magnified JPEG artifacts. Fine for the vomit case (colour/texture/foreign material at arm's length); **marginal-to-useless for 6pt ingredient text**, which is the case the row was originally filed for. Raising the cap is a separate decision with storage/vision-cost/upload-time consequences. Do the gesture work once, with **B-037** (swipe-down-to-dismiss + the tap affordance) — and note the two live gesture conflicts in the existing viewer: pinch inside a horizontal paging `ScrollView`, and single-tap-anywhere-to-dismiss vs. double-tap-to-zoom. Full discussion + options + PM decisions: session 2026-07-26. | Later | 2026-05-26 | — | **Partial — option A shipped 2026-07-26 via PR #461 (PM chose it in-session; session record `docs/sessions/2026-07-26-pinch-zoom-photos.md`).** Pinch + pan now work on all 4 `PhotoViewer` surfaces via RN's **built-in** `ScrollView` zoom — **no new dependency, so it ships OTA** rather than waiting on the W6 native cut. Per-photo ceiling computed from the pixels that actually exist (`lib/photoZoom.ts`, `resolveMaxZoomScale`: `contain` ratio × `PixelRatio` → reciprocal, clamped): a full-sensor local capture gets ~2.5–3× of **real** detail, a remote 1600px photo computes ~1.0–1.4× and is floored. Adds **no tap gesture**, so B-022's single-tap-to-dismiss survives untouched and the double-tap conflict never arises. The media box is now **measured** (`onLayout`) with slides sized in explicit points, which also retires the two flex-collapse-to-black traps the old implementation carried comments about. Intrinsic size is read from the image's own `onLoad`, not `Image.getSize` — the latter would re-fetch a signed URL just to learn dimensions already in flight. 25 new tests (`lib/photoZoom.test.ts` incl. monotonicity + bounds sweeps; `components/ui/PhotoViewer.test.tsx` for the wiring). **Still open:** iOS-only by construction (the zoom props are `ScrollViewPropsIOS`; Android degrades to exactly the previous non-zooming behaviour) → cross-platform pinch, double-tap-to-zoom and swipe-down-to-dismiss are **B-037**'s gesture-handler pass, which should ride the pending native build. **One judgment call awaiting on-device ratification (PM decision #4):** `MIN_MAX_ZOOM_SCALE = 2.5` lets a low-detail remote photo zoom past its real-pixel ceiling into interpolation, because a dead pinch reads as broken and magnification has value for *pointing* in an exam room; Dr. Chen's artifact objection argues for `1`. One constant, documented at the flip point. |

| B-138 | Medication-confounder (PR 9) calibration residuals — dose-point under-detection + incidental-drug over-suppression | Surfaced by the adversarial-reviewer on B-117 PR 9. Two ACCEPTED, safe-direction limitations of the §8 confounder pass — both logged for Data-Scientist/Dr. Chen calibration, neither a code bug, neither ever falsely reassures (the cardinal invariant held): **(a) dose-as-point under-detection** — an administered dose is modeled as a POINT in `[onset−W, onset]` (like a meal), so a once-daily LONG-ACTING, regimen-UNLINKED drug dosed > W hours before onset (e.g. 06:00 dose, 21:00 vomit, 12h vomit window) is invisible → the confounder isn't caught → a false food correlation can surface (UNDER-suppression). Safe direction (never reassurance; degrades to today's no-med behavior). Robust fix = the regimen SPAN, which only exists once a regimen is set up (doses are regimen-unlinked today, B-135); a dose-persistence/tail is the alternative but reintroduces an arbitrary window — a Dr. Chen window-shape call (composes with the B-135 revisit). **(b) incidental-drug over-suppression** — mirroring the free-fed standing cap, a single incidental/PRN dose in any matched window caps the finding at Early; reviewer Monte-Carlo (real beef→vomit pet + ~5 random PRN doses/40d) wrongly SUPPRESSES the real finding ~4.8% and caps it at Early ~55%. Conservative by design, but it bounds how often the pass nicks a real correlation for PRN/chronically-medicated pets (the diet-trial wedge). Decision for Data-Scientist/PM: tune (require a dose persistence/tail; or cap-not-suppress below a dose-count floor) or ratify as the conservative v1. NOTE: the higher-severity Bonferroni family-shrink tier-inflation the same review found was FIXED in PR 9 (`suppressedFamilyCount` keeps withdrawn candidates in the family + regression test), not deferred here. | Later | 2026-06-20 | Step 10 / B-117 PR 9 follow-up | Open — calibration, carries a Data-Scientist/PM call |

| B-178 | Edge Functions don't auto-deploy on merge → silent drift (post-merge deploy gate) | Surfaced 2026-06-24 by a PM-requested edge-function currency audit: `analyze-vomit`'s **B-028 (#220) merged 2026-06-22 but was never deployed** — the live function ran the month-old v4 (old inline write-back, missing the tested `buildAnalysisWriteBack` never-clobber guard) until this session caught + redeployed it (v5). Root cause: merging a PR that changes `supabase/functions/**` does NOT deploy it — deploys are a separate manual MCP step, so an Edge Function silently drifts from `main` until someone notices. Risk class: a merged clinical/extraction fix (e.g. an `analyze-vomit` escalation tweak, a `generate-signal` confounder fix) can sit un-live indefinitely. Options: (a) a CI job that deploys changed functions on merge to `main` (needs a `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` cloud-env secret — weigh the standing credential vs the MCP path); (b) a lightweight **drift check** — periodically (or in `/wrap`) read back each live function's source sha and compare to a fresh bundle of on-disk source, flag mismatches; (c) a manual "did this PR touch an Edge Function? deploy it" checklist line in the merge ritual. Follow-ups routed: B-189 (history tap-through), B-190 (loss-flag glyph-valence input). **PR 4 (History row) BUILT 2026-06-26 (#249)** — `weight_checks` LEFT JOIN in `getTimeline`/`getEventById` (1:1 via UNIQUE `event_id`; soft-delete via the parent `events.deleted_at` filter) + `weight_kg` on `TimelineRow`/`rowToEvent`; `EventRow` renders `${kgToLbs} lbs` as a quiet secondary line (Scale icon already via `EVENT_TYPES`, PR 2); display-only, no verdict; code-reviewer run (one fix applied: `theme.textSM`). **Fast-follow: Patterns-dashboard weight card — shipped via #251 (2026-06-26)** — `components/dashboard/WeightCard.tsx` (prop-driven sibling of the Profile card; same neutral four-layer presentation, shared `describeWeightDelta`/`formatWeightDate` extracted to `lib/weight.ts` so the never-reassure copy can't drift); wired into `app/insights/index.tsx` + `lib/dashboardScreen.ts` as a `weightTrend` descriptor. **Placement (PM deferred to product team): reading-count-aware** — a populated trend leads the health-trajectory/safety cluster (spec §6 group A, the vet-council #1 datum), an empty card is only a logging nudge so it heads the descriptive cluster instead of out-ranking live safety/intake answers (pm-feature-review call); neutral always (priority = ordering only, dashboard uncapped). `selectDashboardState` now counts weight so a weight-only pet isn't cold-start. code-reviewer (ship-ready) + pm-feature-review (multi-reading SHIP-SHAPED, safety bar PASS) run; single-reading date anchor + empty-copy voice fixes folded in. Follow-ups: B-192 (empty-state coherence), B-193 (4px token). |

A gentle, non-judgmental observation ("treats are the majority of Nyx's logged feedings — worth a word with your vet about balance") routes toward care without diagnosing. **Distinct from B-080** (meal-type collapse + diet churn = *structure*; this is *treat load*); compose with B-080/B-070 + the §11 intake-not-preference floor. `nyx-voice` ship-gate (observation, never judgment). | Later | 2026-06-25 | composes with B-080 diet-structure / B-070 staple-washout | Open |
| B-188 | Harden detector ⑦ `activeWeeks` against the calendar-phase "barbell" (PR-2 work) | B-182 PR-1 adversarial review (2026-06-26) BROKE the distribution floor: `activeWeeks = floor((now − onset)/7d)` is **now-anchored**, so a 3-consecutive-day cluster lands in 1 OR 2 buckets by wall-clock phase. Two consequences — (a) a **two-cluster barbell** (3 stale + 3 recent, ~6 quiet weeks between) reaches `activeWeeks=3` via a bucket-edge straddle and fires, the exact two-endpoint/one-cluster shape §4.2/§4.3 claim it excludes; (b) identical data flips fire↔silent across calendar days (jarring on a safety surface). **Safe-direction (over-fire, never reassurance) and only the marginal activeWeeks=3 shape → NOT a merge-blocker; deferred from PR 1.** Fix in **B-182 PR 2** (adversarial-mandatory anyway) with a calendar-independent, straddle-robust distribution measure — note the simple re-anchors (`firstOnset` / fixed-UTC-week) kill the non-determinism but a consecutive-day cluster can still straddle a fixed 7-day edge, so the real fix counts distinct *bouts'* weeks phase-stably. Flag the §4.2 spec edit. | Next | 2026-06-26 | B-182 PR 2 (composition/ranking) | **Done — 2026-06-26 (PR #247)** — fixed in B-182 PR 2 with `countDistributionWeeks`: a phase-stable greedy ≥7-day-gap packing computed from onset data alone (never `now`). Closes both faults — a tight cluster counts as ONE distribution-week so a barbell → 2 (< minActiveWeeks 3, no straddle), and identical data is calendar-phase-invariant. Activity-selection-optimal so no false-negative under-count (adversarial-reviewer swept q5/q6-day courses + a 7-phase barbell regression held). §4.2 spec edit flagged as a Tier-2 doc follow-up (PM-gated, not yet written). |

| B-202 | Weight snapshot (`pets.weight_kg`) direct write has no offline retry | code-reviewer (2026-07-02, B-197): both the log path (`app/log.tsx`) and the new `updateWeightCheck` (`lib/weight.ts`) re-point the denormalized `pets.weight_kg` snapshot via a best-effort direct `supabase.from('pets').update(...)` with no queue/retry — an edit or log made offline silently fails to correct the snapshot, and nothing re-attempts until another weight write recomputes it. The `weight_checks` history rows ARE queued (source of truth), so the trend self-heals; only the denormalized snapshot (profile header / next pre-fill) can lag. Also `updateWeightCheck` writes unconditionally (no `!== current` guard like log.tsx) — a harmless extra call per edit. Fix: route the snapshot through the sync queue (or a small retry) and skip the write when unchanged. Pre-existing in log.tsx; B-197 widened the surface. | Later | 2026-07-02 | — (B-186 robustness) | Open |

Pre-existing (the old post-encode filter did the same) and made rarer by the client-compress fix (an oversized stored original now essentially requires a legacy row), but honesty would set `photoUnreadable` / a "partial" marker when `usableBlobs.length < blobs.length`. Food photos are client-compressed so it's latent, but a large/uncompressed upload would OOM the same way. Handler now drops an un-bringable photo from the set and fails cleanly (`ai_extraction_status='failed'`) only when ALL photos are unusable. 4 new `bytesToBase64` vectors (incl. the 32 KB-boundary oracle test); 29/29 deno tests green; `code-reviewer` ship-ready. `extract-food-from-photo` redeployed live **v12** (verify_jwt preserved, source read-back byte-identical). | B-214 | Vet report: `hoursSinceLastFullMeal` uses UTC instants against a local-day window end | The B-213 "how long off food?" gap is `detNowMs − lastFullMs` (report.ts), UTC instants, while `detectionNowIso` for a **custom past window** is `<endDate>T23:59:59.999Z` (UTC end-of-day of a *local* end-date). In a western tz (e.g. UTC-10), a full meal logged local-evening of the window's end day has a UTC instant *after* that boundary → negative gap → `Math.max(0,…)` floors it to `0 h`. Bounded (≤ tz offset ~14 h), only bites meals genuinely near a custom-window end, and a multi-day gap can never be clamped short — but it under-states (the reassuring direction) for that sliver. Default cascade (window end = now) is unaffected. Fix = anchor the gap (and `detectionNowFor`) to the owner-local end-of-day. Surfaced by `adversarial-reviewer` on the B-213 PR (#263). | Later | 2026-07-03 | — | Open |
| B-215 | Vet report: intake meal-log assumes meal `occurredAt` is witnessed (no B-010 guard) | The B-213 recent-meals appendix (`intakeLogRow`) and the flag's "last full meal" line render meal times/dates as precise points, with no `occurredAtConfidence` guard — correct today only because rated meals are witnessed-by-invariant (the intake lane has no non-witnessed meal path). If a non-witnessed rated meal ever becomes possible, `fmtLocalTime` would print false precision. Latent, not live. Fix = carry meal confidence into the intake path and render a range/estimate like appendix A does for symptoms. Surfaced by `adversarial-reviewer` on the B-213 PR (#263). | Later | 2026-07-03 | — | Open |
| B-216 | Vet report: chronicity flag `activeWeeks` count doesn't reconcile with the span / trend chart | The B-182 chronicity safety flag reads "N episodes across **M weeks**" where M = `activeWeeks` (distinct now-anchored active-week buckets, B-188 phase-stable), but the same page shows a longer span (e.g. "ongoing 36 days") and a trend chart with more populated week-bars (6). Two non-reconciling week-counts on one page invite a vet to doubt the denominators. It **understates** chronicity (safe direction) and doesn't change clinical action. Fix = label it ("across N *separate* weeks") or reconcile the two bucketings. Touches `render.ts:537` (chronicity `flagRow`) — a B-182 render, wants its own small change + Dr. Chen wording sign-off, not a rider on the diet/weight/stool PR. Surfaced by `rls-privacy-reviewer` on PR 5 (#265). | **Next** | 2026-07-03 | **Step 9 PR 6 (public share link) — hard blocker** | **Done — 2026-07-05 (PR #283).** Migration `026_drop_vet_reports_public_share.sql` (renumbered 025→026 to clear a same-number collision with #282's concurrently-merged `025_event_attachments_access_hardening.sql`) DROPs the leaky policy; applied live via the Supabase MCP (`vet_reports` now retains only `vet_reports_owner` → owner-only, RLS still enabled, 0 rows, `get_advisors(security)` clean — no `vet_reports` finding). | B-223 | Dual intake capture — objective intake + subjective owner-perceived-appetite rating | PM idea (2026-07-04, out of the #1 `0/25`-suppress decision): expand dietary-preference data by pairing the existing objective intake question with a *subjective* owner-perceived-appetite rating (high/med/low style — user-friendly but science-backed, ideally a validated appetite-scale instrument). Gives the free-fed/grazer case a real appetite signal instead of only the "intake not directly observed" descriptive line. **Safety constraint for any future spec:** the subjective rating is owner *perception* — it must NEVER reassure over or override an objective decline signal (intake-is-not-preference + n=1-never-reassures); it may add texture or escalate, never soften. Needs research (validated instruments) + schema (rating field on intake/meal capture) + capture-UX. | Later | 2026-07-04 | Food library / intake evolution; feeds the Step 9 free-fed feeding line + Step 10 | Open |
| B-224 | Vet report: the "Reading the trend" (GP-0) confound note should weigh onset-aligned off-diet exposures, not only started interventions | Surfaced by `vet-report-cold-read` on the round-2 artifact (B-221). The GP-0 note is driven by `concurrentChanges` — diet-trial / medication / supplement / free-fed **interventions that started**. On the Nyx report it names the two *constant* free-fed diets (both ongoing since before onset) but omits the exposure that actually tracks symptom onset: the ~340 Temptations treats (May 15–Jun 24, starting the day after first vomiting). Two unchanged diets can't explain a *change*; the onset-aligned treat run can. It is NOT withheld (Appendix B date-span + the staple-washout line carry it), so this is a "point the confound callout at the strong confounder, not just the weak ones" improvement, not a correctness bug. Care needed: treats aren't a started-intervention, so naive inclusion would over-fire on every treat — the right trigger is an off-diet exposure run whose START aligns with (or precedes) symptom onset. Adversarial-mandatory when built (it changes what the highest-consequence note points at). | Later | 2026-07-04 | Step 9 / Step 10 confounder model | Open |

| B-228 | "Ask AI" open-ended chat assistant — competitive-parity feature | PM (2026-07-04): competitors (Lumsters, Companain) now ship an open-ended "Ask [Pet]'s AI anything" chat surface — a free-text box the owner can ask any pet-care question, distinct from Nyx's existing bounded LLM surfaces (AI Signal phrasing = one templated sentence over a pre-computed finding; `extract-food-from-photo`/`extract-medication-from-photo` = structured vision extraction). This would be Nyx's first genuinely **open-ended** conversational LLM surface — no fixed template, no `tool_choice`-forced schema, arbitrary owner questions in, arbitrary model prose out. That is a materially different risk profile from anything shipped so far and needs its own spec before a build-ready decision, not just a UI clone: (1) **n=1-never-reassures / intake-is-not-preference** — a free-text chat is far easier to accidentally reassure through than a template (`validatePhrasing` only guards the templated Signal path; an open chat has no equivalent guardrail yet); (2) **data-minimization** — does the assistant get context (this pet's logged events/photos) or answer generically? If pet-specific, what crosses the LLM boundary (raw logs/photos vs. computed findings only) — same data-minimization gate as the two open council-report Questions below; (3) **scope/wedge fit** — is this the reactive-tracking wedge or a generic pet-care chatbot feature that dilutes it (Designer/PM call); (4) **Pets > $** — if gated behind premium, the safety-relevant answers can't be (Principle 7), same constraint as the gestalt-reviewer/council-report Questions; (5) relationship to the two already-open Questions on LLM-over-findings surfaces ("AI Signals card — scope of any LLM-over-findings surface" and "Surface a council-style multi-perspective report to OWNERS?") — this may be a third rung of that same ladder (bounded finding-phrasing → gestalt reviewer → free-form Q&A) rather than a fully separate feature, and should be scoped together with them rather than designed in isolation. | B-267 | iOS camera + photo-library permission usage strings missing from `app.json` | `expo-image-picker` drives camera (`requestCameraPermissionsAsync`/`launchCameraAsync`) and library (`requestMediaLibraryPermissionsAsync`) across food-capture, medication-capture, incident photos, and pet photos, but `app.json` sets no `NSCameraUsageDescription`/`NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription` (only `ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption`) and does **not** list the `expo-image-picker` config plugin in `plugins` — so no purpose strings are injected at prebuild. iOS **crashes** an app that requests a permission with no purpose string, and App Review rejects a missing/blank one (Guideline 5.1.1). Fix: add `expo-image-picker` to `plugins` with explicit `cameraPermission`/`photosPermission`/`microphonePermission` copy (the plugin sets all three — camera capture can include video; Nyx-voice, states *why* — "so you can add a photo of a meal, symptom, or medication label"; Apple rejects generic "needs camera access" strings), verify the prebuilt Info.plist carries all keys. Verify on the actual build, don't assume EAS autolinking supplies them. **Advisory pass (2026-07-07):** the same built-artifact check should also confirm the aggregated `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` privacy manifest is present (Expo SDK 54 auto-aggregates required-reason declarations; verify, don't assume). | **Now** | 2026-07-07 | First App Store submission / any TestFlight build that hits camera or library | **Config fixed — shipped via #299** (rebased onto the Culprit-rebrand `main`, 2026-07-08): the `expo-image-picker` config plugin now injects `NSCameraUsageDescription` + `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription` with **Culprit**-branded, purpose-stating copy; `microphonePermission=false` suppresses the unused mic string (re-verified on current `main` that every image-picker call site is `mediaTypes: ['images']` — no video capture, so the mic string would be unused). `expo config --type introspect` confirms both keys inject (resolved app name `Culprit`) and the mic key does not. **Scope-limited to the permission strings — did NOT include B-269's `supportsTablet: false` flip**, which shipped separately 2026-07-24 (PR #430); guide step 5 is now complete across both halves. **Residuals (PM/on-build, introspect can't check either):** (1) verify both keys on the actual prebuilt/EAS `Info.plist`; (2) confirm the aggregated `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` privacy manifest is present (Expo SDK 54 auto-aggregates — verify, don't assume). |

| B-341 | Context-adaptive intake timing — resolve a grazed meal's intake at the *next* feeding, not at log time | The WSAVA intake chips fire co-located with the meal log (`MealCompletionCard`), which is the right moment for **scarfers** (dog kibble, cat treats — consumption ≈ instantaneous) but **premature for grazers** (cat wet food): the owner doesn't know how much was eaten until the next feeding, when they see the leftover bowl. A single co-located capture point is structurally biased against cats (Dr. Chen's "single `offered_at`" bias, `2026-05-feeding-windows` §6) → the highest-value `feline_reduced_intake` flag (B-027/B-033) stays silent for exactly the pet most at risk, because the owner skips/guesses/forgets the premature ask. Proposed: a **"eaten over time / grazed" property on the (pet, food) pair** (food-domain toggle, sibling to free-choice B-040; sensible default inferred from species + `food_type`), a **pending-intake state that never assumes finished** (B-156 G1 fail-safe generalized), and **resolution at the next-meal ritual** ("answering beats initiating", `logging-capture-discovery` §2 — zero new nag). Honest modelling: consumed-at is a *window* (offered→assessed), not a point → ties to B-010 `occurred_at_confidence` + the free-feeding "intake not directly observed" caveat for acute correlation. **This is the wet-food offered-vs-consumed timing window the free-feeding R1 explicitly fenced off** (`nyx-free-feeding-requirements.md` §0) — Eng blast-radius flag: touches the meal/event model, not a pure additive edge. Safety: a grazer leaving wet food is the canonical false-reassurance trap — resolution copy routes refused/picked toward a health flag, never softens to "picky" (intake-is-not-preference). | Next | 2026-07-13 | Composes with B-014 / B-015 / B-033 / B-040; ties to the 2026-07-10 "are owner-configured confirmations nudges?" Open Question | Open — surfaced 2026-07-13 (PM dogfooding: Nyx grazes wet food, assessed at the next feeding). Persona + product-team discussion held this session. **PM direction 2026-07-13:** (1) **in-app resolve-at-next-log endorsed as the core**; PM also wants to explore **multi-channel** (in-app **+** a notification, possibly staggered timing) for a higher resolution/conversion rate than any single channel — endorsed *with two non-negotiables baked in*: **cross-channel de-dup** (a resolution in one channel silences the other — never nag about an already-answered bowl) and **fail-safe silence** (an ignored notification records *nothing*, never "finished" — B-156 G1). The *notification* channel inherits the deferred **2026-07-10 Open Question** (are owner-configured confirmations "nudges" under Principle 4's cap?); the in-app ride does not. (2) **Property carrier** (how the app knows a food is "eaten over time") left as a **build-time decision**, not blocking — team lean: **infer a smart default** from species + `food_type`, **one-time owner-correctable toggle** in the food domain (sibling to free-choice), avoid a per-instance "check back later" (re-adds a moment-of-event decision). (3) WSAVA explainer split to **B-342**; "go deeper later." Discovery doc (`nyx-intake-timing-requirements.md`) deferred until the roadmap slot — PM: go deeper later, if needed. |

| B-279 | Harden the chunked SecureStore auth adapter — concurrency + orphan bound | Two non-blocking residuals from the #306 `code-reviewer` re-review of `lib/secureStore.ts` (the frequent-signin fix). **(1) No concurrency mutex:** two overlapping `setItem` calls on the same key (e.g. an auto-refresh write racing a user sign-in) could both compute `prev.gen + 1` and collide on one generation's key set, reintroducing a mixing risk *between two live writers* (distinct from the write+crash case, which IS fixed). Safe in practice only because supabase-js typically serializes its own storage calls during refresh — an external assumption this module doesn't defend. Add an in-module per-key write queue if session-persistence bugs ever resurface under rapid sign-in/out/refresh interleaving. **(2) Unbounded generation counter:** `gen` increments forever and cleanup is best-effort, so a *sustained* run of cleanup failures would accumulate orphaned chunk keys indefinitely (never read — the pointer names only the live gen — so correctness-safe, storage-only). Low real-world likelihood; only worth a pass if the file is reopened. | Later | 2026-07-08 | — (only if session-persistence bugs resurface under concurrent auth ops) | Open — **the "if session-persistence bugs resurface" trigger HAS fired**: frequent logouts confirmed still-present on **build 32** (#306 did NOT fix them; #327 shipped on-device instrumentation to find the real cause — server logs show cold-start session-restore failure on iOS). The concurrency-mutex residual (#1) is a leading fix candidate, but this whole row may be **superseded** if the #327 trail points to reverting the chunked adapter to a verified single-key one. Originally surfaced by the #306 code-reviewer re-review (2026-07-08); both residuals non-blocking + correctness-safe. **Update 2026-07-15: the resurfaced logout was DIAGNOSED (build-33 trail) as keychain accessibility — `WHEN_UNLOCKED` blocked the locked-device background refresh — NOT the concurrency residual, and FIXED in #350 (`AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK`). The chunked adapter is RETAINED, not reverted (the "revert to single-key" speculation above is moot — chunking was confirmed working in the same trail). The two original residuals (#1 concurrency mutex, #2 orphan bound) remain Open + non-blocking; revisit only if logouts persist after build 34.** |
| B-366 | Reconcile the library (format-blind) vs archive (format-aware) grouping seam | Surfaced by `pm-feature-review` on B-005 PR 3 (2026-07-17). The Foods-tab library groups **format-blind** (`GROUP BY brand, product_name` — one tile per food), but `archiveFood` collects duplicates **by format** (`.eq('format', row.format)`) and `ARCHIVED_FOODS_QUERY` groups by format too. For a food captured in **two formats** (e.g. a prescription diet in dry + wet — a wedge case), tapping "Remove from library" archives only the tapped format's captures: the format-blind library tile survives on the other format, so the food shows in **both** the library (still-active format) AND the Archived section (removed format), reading as "a removal that didn't take" + a phantom archived row. Each side is internally consistent; the two grouping models contradict at the seam. **PM decision:** pick one model for both (make `archiveFood`/`restoreFood`/`ARCHIVED_FOODS_QUERY` format-blind to match the library, OR make the removal copy explain it removes one format), then reconcile. Format-aware was chosen for PR 3 because it gives each archived row a clean single-stamp restore-unit; going format-blind means restore must clear multiple (format, stamp) units per tile. Needs a device pass with a genuinely two-format food. | Next | 2026-07-17 | B-005 archive UX coherence; multi-format foods (prescription diets) | Open — surfaced 2026-07-17 (pm-feature-review, B-005 PR 3). |

Consider queueing these flag-flips through the offline sync path, or at minimum a clearer offline-specific message. | Later | 2026-07-17 | Per-account catalog offline story | Open — surfaced 2026-07-17 (pm-feature-review, B-005 PR 3). |

| B-390 | Minimal CI workflow (tsc + jest on PRs) | Today all 379 merges land on `main` with 0 automated checks; the only gate is the local, bypassable `.githooks/pre-push`. Add `.github/workflows/ci.yml` running `npx tsc --noEmit` + `npm test` on PRs — a server-side floor under the ~9-PR/day AI cadence. **Behavior change (starts gating merges) → explicit PM yes; confirm the deno-excluded suites + install steps pass in CI first.** | Next | 2026-07-19 | CI floor under AI cadence | **Done — 2026-07-25 (PR #440).** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` + an Active `main` ruleset — the repo's first server-side gate after ~400 unchecked merges. **Workflow:** job `app` (`npm ci` → `tsc --noEmit` → `jest --ci`, 110 suites / 1688 cases) + job `edge-functions` (`deno test` over `supabase/functions`, 829 cases — the deno-excluded suites the pre-push hook never ran, and the only type check covering that path since `tsconfig.json` excludes it). Actions SHA-pinned, `contents: read`, network confined to one retried `deno cache` step with the assertion step on `--cached-only`. **Gate (the behaviour change B-390 required an explicit PM yes for — given 2026-07-25):** ruleset `main`, Enforcement **Active**, target = default branch, **bypass list empty** (nobody bypasses, incl. the owner — the Rulesets equivalent of classic's "do not allow bypassing"), both checks required, PR required at **0 approvals** (correct for a solo maintainer — 1 would self-lock), require-branches-up-to-date, block-force-pushes, restrict-deletions. A red check now blocks the merge. **Flakiness question answered with real data:** on 2026-07-25 `esm.sh` returned **HTTP 522** for ~2.5 min and reddened the deno job — the retry worked as designed (3 tries, then a loud honest error) but 30s of backoff couldn't outlast the outage. Fixed at the exposure rather than by weakening the check: **`DENO_DIR` is now cached** (`actions/cache`, keyed on `deno.lock`), so a hit means the job touches the network **zero** times and the CDN is only on the path when the lockfile changes; retry widened to 4×(10/20/40s) with a per-attempt `timeout` so a *stalled* fetch can't sit on one attempt for the whole job. `continue-on-error` deliberately NOT used — that would trade the 829-case gate for green. **Found en route → B-434** (stale `deno.lock` workspace block blocks `--frozen`). One residual, non-blocking: allowed merge methods are still Merge/Squash/Rebase while the house rule is squash-only — see B-433. |

The scope-end anchor may well be deliberate for a historical report — that is exactly the call to make explicitly rather than inherit. | B-443 | Ask's trial day counter is only as good as `user_profiles.timezone`, which defaults to New York | B-421 made `ask/tools.ts dietTrialStatus` bucket by the owner's stored zone — correct when the zone is correct. Two gaps remain, and the reachable one is not the one the code guards. **(a)** The fallback asymmetry: the client's fallback is the DEVICE zone, the server's is UTC, so a Sydney owner with no stored zone sees card **Day 5** / Ask **Day 4**. **(b)** The reachable case: `timezone` is `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'America/New_York'` (migration 001), so a profile whose `stampTimezone` never ran never *reaches* the fallback — it buckets a Kolkata owner's day by New York and disagrees with the card for ~10.5h of every day, with nothing in the code aware anything is wrong. Fix is upstream of the counter: guarantee the zone is stamped (and consider whether a never-stamped profile should be distinguishable from a genuine New York owner — a `NOT NULL DEFAULT` cannot express "unknown"). | B-434 | `deno.lock` workspace block is stale (pre-SDK-57), so CI can't use `--frozen` | Found building B-390 (PR #440). `deno.lock`'s `workspace.dependencies` still mirrors the **pre-SDK-57** `package.json` (`expo@~54.0.35`, `jest-expo@54.0`, `babel-preset-expo@~54.0.10`, `@types/react@19.1`, …), so Deno rewrites the lockfile on every invocation and `deno cache --frozen` **fails on a clean checkout** (reproduced). CI therefore runs plain `--lock=deno.lock` — which still verifies every remote file against its committed hash (the part with security value: a `deno.land`/`esm.sh` URL whose content silently changed fails the build) but cannot catch lockfile drift. Note a bare regenerate does **not** stick: the next `package.json` bump re-staleifies the block, and `--frozen` would then red every dependency PR until someone remembered to re-run Deno. So the fix wants to be self-enforcing — either (a) regenerate + a CI step that fails if `deno.lock` is dirty after the run, or (b) stop tracking the npm workspace in `deno.lock` at all, since the Edge Functions never import `npm:` specifiers from `package.json`. Decide before turning `--frozen` on. | Later | 2026-07-25 | CI `--frozen` posture (B-390) | Open — filed 2026-07-25 (PR #440); plain `--lock` integrity checking is the interim mitigation. |
| B-433 | `main` ruleset still allows Merge/Squash/Rebase while the house rule is squash-only | Spotted reviewing the 2026-07-25 ruleset (PR #440 follow-up). The Git Workflow rule says *"Squash merge to keep `main` history clean and linear"* — a written convention the new ruleset can now **enforce**, since Rulesets exposes an **Allowed merge methods** dropdown. It's currently left at all three, so a stray "Create a merge commit" still produces exactly the non-linear history the rule exists to prevent. Fix: ruleset `main` → *Require a pull request before merging* → **Allowed merge methods = Squash** only. Note this also makes `Require linear history` redundant (squash can't create a merge commit), so don't tick both and expect them to mean different things. Pure repo setting — no code, no PR; ~15 seconds. | Later | 2026-07-25 | Enforcing the existing squash-merge convention | Open — cosmetic vs. the B-390 gate itself, which is live and enforcing; filed so the written rule and the enforced rule converge. |
| B-435 | Backlog ID allocation races between parallel sessions | Observed **three times on 2026-07-25**: this session filed B-418 then B-432 (that B-432 is now **B-487**) and had to rename both across five files, and `main`'s own B-152 session hit it independently (its row records "filed as B-417 mid-session, renumbered to B-432 — B-417 was taken on `main`"). The contract says *"sequential `B-NNN`. Never reuse"*, but allocation is *read the max, add one* against a working copy — a lost-update race the moment two sessions are open, and this repo runs ~9 PRs/day. It fails **silently**: two rows share an ID and every cross-reference to it becomes ambiguous; the only reason all three were caught is that a merge conflict forced a re-read. Options: **(a)** a `/wrap`-time duplicate-ID check that fails loudly (`grep -ohE '^\| B-[0-9]+ ' docs/backlog.md \| sort \| uniq -d` — a few lines, would have caught all three *before* push); **(b)** reserve a block of IDs per session so parallel sessions cannot overlap; **(c)** abandon sequential IDs for something collision-free. Recommend **(a)** now, **(b)** if the cadence keeps rising. | Next | 2026-07-25 | Backlog integrity under parallel sessions | Done — 2026-07-26 (PR #473). Option **(a)** shipped: `/wrap` Step 4 runs the `uniq -d` check and treats any output as stop-and-fix before commit, with a written resolution rule (**first row on `main` keeps the ID, later arrival renumbers**), an attribute-don't-blind-replace instruction for cross-references, and the inline-provenance convention. Also cleared the backlog it was filed against: **five** live collisions, not the three recorded here — B-432/B-441/B-442/B-443 plus B-477 (2026-07-26) — renumbered to **B-487–B-491**, keeping the ID on the row that landed first in every case (which is also the row code/tests already referenced). **The rule then bit its own author:** merging `main` at wrap showed a sibling build-35 QA session had taken B-481–B-485 — the very block this session had just renumbered *into* — so all five moved again, first-lands-keeps applied against ourselves. Five silent collisions became ten, and the check caught every one. Option **(b)** (per-session ID blocks) deliberately not taken: the check makes the race *loud*, which was the actual failure, and a reservation scheme adds coordination for a collision that now costs one renumber. |
| B-486 | 134 `Open` rows sit inside the `## Done` table — the two sections no longer mean anything | Found while fixing B-435 (2026-07-26). `docs/backlog.md` has two tables, `## Open` (262 rows) and `## Done` (218) — but **134 rows below the `## Done` header carry `Open` status**, because new rows have long been appended to the *end of the file* rather than to the Open table (B-467–B-472, B-444–B-449, and four of the five rows this session renumbered — B-488–B-491 — all landed that way; only B-487 was filed into the Open table). So section membership carries no information and the `Status` column is the only truth, which is survivable but actively misleading: a session skimming for what's left reads the wrong table, and `view backlog` grouping by section would misreport. It is also a contributing cause of B-435's ID races — two sessions appending to two different regions of one file don't conflict, so nothing forced a re-read. Fix is a grooming pass that moves rows to the section matching their `Status` head, then a companion `/wrap` assertion (no `Open` row below the `## Done` header) in the same shape as the duplicate-ID check — deliberately **not** added now, because it would fail on all 134 rows on the first run. Cheap but a wide diff on the file every parallel session touches, so it wants its own quiet PR, not a ride-along. **Fold in one adjacent mechanical defect while in there** (same file, same vehicle, so composing rather than a second ID): **10 rows carry a raw unescaped `\|` inside a cell** (B-081 has six), which ends that row's table early and drops the trailing columns — including `Status` — in any strict renderer. | B-507 | Migration history and on-disk filenames use two different version schemes — `db push` from disk is still unsafe | _(Filed as B-494; **renumbered to B-507** at wrap — B-494 was taken on `main` by the cat-refusing-meals vet-report row.)_ The B-162 backfill closed less of this than the row assumed. `schema_migrations.version` holds **26 14-digit timestamps** (written by MCP `apply_migration`, e.g. `20260725180333 diet_trial_lifecycle`) while the files are `NNN_name.sql` (`040_diet_trial_lifecycle.sql` ⇒ version `040`). The CLI compares local file versions against that column, so **not one** of those 26 files is recognised as applied; the backfill only fixed the 18 it recorded (matched files 0/44 → 18/44). A bare `supabase db push` would still try to re-run 26 migrations — several with bare `CREATE TYPE` / `CREATE TABLE` / `ADD COLUMN` that error or partially apply, and some (e.g. `006`, were it in that set) would *re-open* RLS holes later migrations closed. Every new `apply_migration` adds one more mismatch (a sibling's `vet_documents` did exactly that mid-session). Options for the PM/Eng: **(a)** `UPDATE` the 26 rows' `version` to the filename prefix — one row per change, keeps `created_by`, loses the applied-at timestamp encoded in `version`; **(b)** add a *second* row per file keyed on the prefix — safe for `db push` but duplicates history; **(c)** rename the 26 files to their recorded timestamps — breaks the `NNN` convention that docs, PRs and CLAUDE.md reference everywhere; **(d)** accept it — the documented deploy path is the MCP, not `db push`, so treat history as documentation/drift-detection only and never run `db push`. (d) is the status quo and is defensible; it just needs to be a *decision* rather than an accident, and if chosen, `db push` should be explicitly warned against in the deploy runbook. | Later | 2026-07-26 | Any future `supabase db push` / CLI-from-disk flow; B-506 | Open |

So it's "safe by entropy," not "safe by construction." Fix (own schema/RLS PR + `rls-privacy-reviewer`, mirroring B-244): a `WITH CHECK` / `BEFORE INSERT` binding `pet_id IN (SELECT id FROM pets WHERE user_id = auth.uid())` **and** `storage_path LIKE pet_id || '/%'`. | B-253 | Public vet-report share link — deferred (Supabase serves HTML as text/plain); rebuild PDF-based | **PM decision 2026-07-06: defer the public browser link.** The v1 sharing mechanism is the working **"Send to vet" → on-device PDF → native share sheet** (PR 5/7); the public link was always secondary (spec §8.2). **Why deferred — a hard platform constraint found in live testing (not a code bug):** Supabase serves `text/html` as **`text/plain`** from BOTH Edge Functions (docs: "GET requests that return text/html will be rewritten to text/plain") AND Storage (docs: "HTML files are returned as plain text") as an anti-XSS/phishing measure — so a browser-viewable **HTML** report link served from Supabase is impossible as spec §7 assumed ("stream the artifact / short-lived signed URL"). **Future rebuild = PDF-based** (browsers render PDFs inline from a signed Storage URL; only HTML is special-cased): mint = the client's on-device `expo-print` PDF (the SAME trusted artifact as the share sheet) uploaded to `nyx-vet-reports` + a `vet_reports` token row; `view-report` = validate token + 30-day expiry → **302 → short-lived (~60s) signed PDF URL** (§8-compliant, never a baked long-TTL URL). The security design from the reverted build holds (both gates passed: `rls-privacy-reviewer` = PASS, `code-reviewer` folded) — reuse its token-validation/expiry logic + the B-218 posture; the reverted HTML mint/`view-report` source lives in PR #285's history. **Composes with B-254** (bind `vet_reports` INSERT `pet_id`/`storage_path`) + **B-255** (reap expired artifacts) when built. **Deployed-but-inert cleanup:** the `view-report` v1 (verify_jwt=false) function was deployed during investigation and is now a no-op (nothing mints → always 404; harmless — B-218 closed anon enumeration) — delete it via the dashboard at leisure, or leave it. | Later | 2026-07-06 | Step 9 public link (revisit post-MVP / when prioritized) | Open — deferred by PM 2026-07-06; the platform constraint is recorded here so it is never re-litigated. B-250 (its schema dep) shipped + applied to prod. |

| B-280 | "Forgot password?" / password-reset path on login | The app has NO password-recovery flow anywhere (grep: no `resetPasswordForEmail`), so a returning owner who forgets their password is dead-ended on the one screen meant to let them back to their pet's history — a hard "no dead ends" violation (onboarding spec §6/§10) and a store-readiness gap (a login-gated app really needs reset before submission). Surfaced by `pm-feature-review` of the login rebuild (#311); pre-existing, not introduced by that PR, but the consolidation is the natural moment to add it: a "Forgot password?" link under the password field → `supabase.auth.resetPasswordForEmail` → a calm "check your inbox" state (reuse signup's verify-state shape) + a deep-link recovery handler. | Now | 2026-07-09 | Store-readiness / first App Store submission | In progress — **PR 1 of 5 Done — 2026-07-25 (PR #444)**: foundations shipped (`flowType:'pkce'`, the pure `lib/passwordRecovery.ts` two-stage FR-4 classification, the **persisted** FR-6 recovery gate, the FR-12/FR-14 request marker, FR-17 log redaction, `PASSWORD_RECOVERY_ENABLED=false`) — no user-visible change, `code-reviewer` ship-ready, 88 new tests. **Remaining: PR 2** (the whole flow; gated only on the §9.3 device checks — all PM rulings landed) · **PR 3** (settings change-password; buildable now) · **PR 4** (enablement; its B-152 SMTP gate LIFTED 2026-07-25 by #445, now gated on PR 2 + the §9.2 dashboard row) · **PR 5** (email change, D9; slot open). Two PR-1 calls recorded for PR 2: FR-4(b) ships **three** exchange outcomes not four (GoTrue returns one indistinguishable shape for expired/used/resend-overwritten, and a type claiming otherwise invites copy asserting a cause the device cannot know), with an optional `verifierPresent` so PR 2 is correct either way §9.3-Q2 lands; and §6.4's wipe clears the FR-12 marker **before** the exchange, so the pre-fill must come from memory, not a disk re-read. Earlier — **2026-07-25 (PR #437)**: requirements + design session complete. Spec **v1.2** + design-locked mocks (`docs/nyx-password-recovery-requirements.md`, `docs/culprit-password-recovery-mockups.html`); 4-PR plan. **PR 1 (foundations — pkce, pure link parser, recovery flag) buildable today**; **PR 2** (the whole flow) gated on **D1c**; **PR 3** (settings change-password) on **D4**; **PR 4** (enablement) on **B-152 SMTP**. Two traps found by reading `app/_layout.tsx` against the Supabase flow and now load-bearing in the spec: (1) a recovery session is adopted unconditionally by the root listener, landing the owner on Home with their password still unknown → the FR-6 recovery gate; (2) a recovery link for account B tapped on a device signed in as A swaps the session while local SQLite + the App Group + the **widget timeline on the Home Screen** still hold A's pet data → the FR-7 `wipeLocalSession()` ordering. Elevated Next→Now by the 2026-07-20 hardening audit §B2. **Three gates ran on v1.0 (spec §12): `nyx-voice` ✓, `pm-feature-review` 2 SHIP-SHAPED / 2 NEEDS-WORK / 1 INSUFFICIENT, and `rls-privacy-reviewer` **FAIL with 5 merge blockers** — all folded into v1.1.** The blockers shared one root cause worth carrying forward: `wipeLocalSession()` was written for the `SIGNED_OUT` transition (session → **null**), but a recovery swap is non-null → non-null, so the session-keyed producers (`useWidgetSnapshots`, `useSync`) **re-arm instead of unmounting** and re-publish the previous owner's pets to the App Group + Home Screen widget *after* the wipe. §6.4 now forces a real `signOut()` before the exchange and reuses the shipped teardown. Also fixed: the gate must be **persisted** (in-memory died on force-quit while the recovery session persisted), cleared in a `finally` (a failed exchange wedged the app), gated on **local provenance** (`nyx:///reset-password?code=x` is firable by any app), and given a **router enforcement point** (none exists — widget deep links walked past it). Files **B-426**/**B-427**/**B-428**/**B-429**; annotates B-401. **PM rulings 2026-07-25: D1c ACCEPTED** (custom-scheme redirect ships; the https interstitial stays B-426, post-submission) · **D4 YES** (password change = PR 3) **and email change scoped in as D9** (spec'd, build slot open → PR 5; corrected a wrong v1 claim about the vet report keying on the account email) · **D6b still OPEN** — PM asked what App Review and best practice say before ruling. Answer (spec §7.1): App Review has **no** guideline here and cannot test it, so it is a product/security call, not a compliance one; but the **standard control is to terminate other sessions on reset**, and the original "no" recommendation never checked it. **Team recommendation therefore CHANGED to "yes on reset (default), optional on change"** — a reset that evicts nothing leaves an attacker's refresh token renewing indefinitely while the owner believes they fixed it. Two conditions attach: the evicted device needs a designed "you were signed out because your password changed" state (reusing the B-039 one-shot banner), and the password-changed notification email must be on. |

| B-322 | `CulpritMark` (N2) pulse likely doesn't animate on device — same native-driver gap N3 fixed | The N3 `code-reviewer` traced react-native-svg's native prop surface: `G`/`RNSVGGroup` exposes only `matrix` as a native prop (not `rotation`/`scale`/`originX/Y`), and the `rotation`/`scale`→`matrix` conversion happens only in G's JS `render()`/`setNativeProps()` — both bypassed when `useNativeDriver:true`. So `CulpritMark`'s live-pulse (an `AnimatedG` `scale` + ping `AnimatedG` driven with `useNativeDriver:true`, `components/brand/CulpritMark.tsx`) probably never reaches the native thread → renders frozen at its static frame on a real build (Fabric/`newArchEnabled:true`), even though `.start()`/tests pass. | B-335 | Adherence chips below the 44pt tap floor when they are the sole safety-resolution surface | `code-reviewer` (B-325). `AdherenceChipRow`'s `Chip` has ~27px effective tap height and no `hitSlop` — pre-existing (reused unchanged in `MedicationCompletionCard` + `event/[id].tsx`), NOT a B-325 regression, but B-325's `ComboDoseConfirmSheet` is the first place the chip row is the *entire* resolution affordance for a safety-in-doubt dose in a modal. A blunt `hitSlop` is unsafe here — the chips are horizontally packed (gap 6), so overlapping touch areas would make a tap between "Given" and "Partial" ambiguous on a safety surface. Fix carefully: raise `paddingVertical` toward the 44pt floor (visual change across all three usages — Designer sign-off) rather than hitSlop, OR give the sheet its own larger-target chip layout. | Later | 2026-07-12 | Revisit with a Designer pass on `AdherenceChipRow` sizing | Open |
| B-336 | Double-tap on a medication tile writes two dose events (no submit guard) | `code-reviewer` (B-325). `MedicationPicker`/`handlePickMedication` has no double-submit guard — a rapid double-tap writes two doses. Pre-existing on the standalone + forward-combo paths; B-325 adds one wrinkle: on the retroactive path the second `setComboConfirm` overwrites the first, so the first dose's confirm sheet never shows (it still resurfaces via the History "Unconfirmed" tag — no data loss, no false "given"). Fix once, at the shared write path: a short in-flight guard / disable-on-first-tap on the picker tiles (also covers the food picker). | Later | 2026-07-12 | Combo + standalone dose-logging paths | **Done — 2026-07-26 (shipped via #471).** Fixed at the shared write path as the row recommended, not on the tiles: `hooks/useSubmitGuard.ts` latches on the first tap and drops every tap that lands while the write is in flight, wired around BOTH `handlePickFood` and `handlePickMedication` in `app/log.tsx`. Release is explicit rather than inferred — the guarded handler returns `true` when it COMMITTED an event (stay latched; the screen is dismissing) and `false` when it wrote nothing (release, so a failed write is retryable), and a throw releases too. Deliberately NOT a `disabled` prop on the tiles: `FoodPicker` also serves the B-417 selection surface (`StartTrialModal`) where re-tapping a tile is the whole point, so guarding the write guards exactly the paths that write. The B-325 wrinkle closes with it — the second tap never reaches `setComboConfirm`, so the first dose's confirm sheet is no longer overwritten. 8 unit tests (`hooks/useSubmitGuard.test.ts`) incl. the burst case, the different-tile case (a fat-fingered second tap usually lands on the neighbour, which a per-tile guard would miss), and the re-render case. A falsification pass found one path back to the bug and produced a second commit: the guard releases on a throw (so a PRE-write failure leaves the picker usable), which meant a POST-write throw — the completion card, the optimistic prepend, `setComboConfirm`, `router.back()` — would release a guard whose event was already on disk, and the next tap would write a second dose for the same pill. Both handlers now settle the answer ON the write and wrap everything after it; a failed card is cosmetic and self-corrects (History + the dose detail read ground truth). The B-156 PR B3 intake→adherence coupling, the write-time pet identity and the B-325 sheet gating are byte-for-byte unchanged. Sibling hole left open + filed as B-491 (filed as B-477; renumbered twice on ID collisions). Session record: `docs/sessions/2026-07-26-picker-double-submit-guard.md`. |

| B-338 | analyze-vomit: separate scene objects from vomit contents (a false-positive CLASS, not a detune) | PM n=1 (2026-07-13): two AI foreign-material reads were both wrong — a blue ball that was a **toy elsewhere in the frame** (not in the vomit at all) and a "stick" that was the **seasoning layer of turkey deli meat**. The first is a genuine prompt/segmentation bug (a scene object read as content); the second is a fine-grained vision limit. **Direction constraint (non-negotiable):** we deliberately escalate-on-presence — false positives are the SAFE error — and B-042 pushes for *more* sensitivity, so this is NOT a licence to detune. Scope narrowly to the safe fix only: prompt the model to distinguish "material that is IN the vomit" from "objects elsewhere in the photo," and/or a confirm-screen affordance to mark scene-only objects; leave sensitivity to material actually in the vomit untouched. `adversarial-reviewer` + `clinical-guardrails` mandatory — must not open an under-escalation path (a real foreign body is an emergency). | Next | 2026-07-13 | — | Open — surfaced 2026-07-13 (PM n=1); pairs with B-042 (opposite direction — read together). |
| B-339 | Stale `visual_flags`/`recommendation` cache after an owner edit (override propagation) | PM n=1 (2026-07-13): overrode the false foreign-material flag (B-028 editable fields). **Verified this session:** the client edit (`buildVomitEditWrite`, `lib/analysis.ts:152`) writes ONLY the owner-editable structured fields + `edited_at` — it deliberately does NOT recompute the cached `visual_flags` / `recommendation` / `read_text`. Consequence split: **(a) the vet report is ALREADY override-aware** — `generate-report` derives a photo's safety class from the structured fields (`foreign_material_present`/`blood_present`/`edited_at`) and never `SELECT`s `visual_flags` (`index.ts:724`), so a cleared flag drops the photo from the safety lead by construction (a sent PDF is a snapshot, so only edits BEFORE generation reach it — not a bug). **(b) the row's cached `visual_flags`/`recommendation` stay STALE** — still `suspected_foreign_material` / `worth_a_call` after the owner clears the fact. Anything keying off those is NOT override-aware: today the event DETAIL screen recommendation badge (minor, escalate-safe / firm), and — critically — the future Signal elevation (B-340). Fix options: recompute `visual_flags`/`recommendation` from the edited structured fields on the edit write (fights the never-clobber stance), OR make consumers derive from structured fields (the report's model — preferred). **B-340 adopts derive-from-structured-fields, so it needs no cache fix; this row is the residual detail-screen staleness + a general "the cache lies after an edit" cleanup.** | Next | 2026-07-13 | Detail-screen recommendation staleness; consistency with B-340 | Open — verified 2026-07-13; report is fine, cache is stale. |

Build a new safety-class detector that reads the pet's in-window non-deleted vomit incidents' AI analysis and fires a firm-but-calm Home safety card when a VISUAL red flag is present, led at top. **Non-negotiable build guardrails:** (1) derive the flag from the owner-editable structured fields (`foreign_material_present==='yes'`; `blood_present∈{fresh_red,coffee_ground}`), NOT the stale `visual_flags` array — so an owner override clears the card by construction (mirrors `generate-report`; see B-339); (2) escalate-on-presence, never reassure (a cleared/absent flag is silence, never an all-clear — `clinical-guardrails`); (3) tone = existing firm safety register, no new "danger" state; (4) single-incident fires (no corroboration gate — false positives are cheap for the owner to clear). **Scope (Engineer rec):** visual flags only (the net-new datum); the contextual `worth_a_call` escalations (repeated-vomiting / feline-reduced-intake / lethargy) are a separate lane question, not this item. **Reads a NEW source into `detectSignals` → `adversarial-reviewer` MANDATORY.** **Deploy-gated:** the client (`lib/signal.ts` InsightType + `InsightCard` renderer) must render the new type BEFORE `generate-signal` is redeployed (the B-182 "registered but unrenderable" lesson). | B-343 | `food_items` global-catalog write is not ownership-checked (any authed user can overwrite any row) | Surfaced by the `rls-privacy-reviewer` during T2-3 (2026-07-14) as a **pre-existing, non-T2-3** finding. `extract-food-from-photo` (and the client commit path) can drive a service-role UPDATE of ANY global `food_items` row given only a body `food_item_id` — the table is globally scoped by design (no `pet_id`/`user_id`; the sanctioned shared-catalog exception), so there is no ownership predicate on the row. | B-345 | Strict ship-dark for the paywall gate during the first-run config-load race (T2-5) | `useAppConfig` resolves `paywall_enabled` asynchronously (auth-driven fetch + cached last-known-good). Because the paywall flag alone fails **closed** (unlike the fail-open AI flags), a brand-new signup with no cache that reaches `app/onboarding/pet-age.tsx` before the config fetch lands reads the `false` default and routes past the seeded-`true` mocked paywall. Per the T2-5 AC this is the "config-unreachable → hidden" clause (not a byte-identical break of the steady state — returning users hydrate from cache instantly, and the single SELECT beats five screens of typing on any real network), and the failure is soft (a mock CTA shown less often, never a dead/broken screen) and degrades toward the eventual submission goal — so it was **accepted as a residual**, not fixed. If the PM later wants strict byte-identical even for that first-run-in-flight window, the fix is to expose a `configReady`/hydrated boolean from `useAppConfig` and have onboarding treat "not yet resolved" explicitly — but note a naive "default to on while loading" would re-open the fail-closed rejection-risk in the other direction, so any fix must default to **hidden-until-resolved** and only *delay* the paywall decision, not force it on. Low stakes: this is a mock with no purchase path until T3-D un-mocks it. | Later | 2026-07-15 | T3-D paywall un-mock (matters more once the paywall is real) | Open — surfaced 2026-07-15 (code-reviewer, T2-5; accepted soft residual). |
