| B-351 | Structured multi-protein capture + set-membership correlation (beyond `primary_protein`) | Surfaced by the PM during B-332 (2026-07-15). Today the food model carries a SINGLE `primary_protein` (extraction-only until B-332; owner-editable after), and the flagship diet correlation keys off that one field. **Dr. Chen:** for the elimination / novel-protein trial — our wedge — hidden *secondary* proteins are the single most common reason a home trial silently fails (a "duck" food that also lists chicken by-product meal contaminates the trial), so "primary only" systematically misses the exposure that matters most; the advisors would favour capturing ALL proteins. **Data Scientist (counterweight):** not a free win — a multi-protein exposure model is harder to attribute (co-occurring proteins are collinear, foods with 4–5 proteins bloat the exposure set, per-protein effective-n drops); it raises *sensitivity to hidden exposure* but muddies *which* protein is the culprit. Done properly this is ONE cross-cutting change — extraction emits all proteins → schema holds them (`proteins` jsonb/array or a join table; NOT `primary_protein` alone) → the correlation engine (`lib/analytics.ts`, `generate-signal/detection.ts`) keys on set-membership → the vet report renders them — and likely absorbs **B-048** (ingredient→protein canonicalization). Note the full ingredient list is ALREADY captured as free text (`food_items.ingredients_notes`), just not structured, so no raw data is being lost today. Deliberately NOT folded into B-332 (which is single-protein parity-with-extraction to unblock the T3-E gate). Gated on the CLAUDE.md Open Question (vet-advisor + PM: sensitivity vs. attribution). `adversarial-reviewer` mandatory (statistical) + Dr. Chen. | Now | 2026-07-15 | Vet-report protein exposure; Step 10 correlation quality; absorbs B-048 | **Done — 2026-07-26 (PR #458) — ALL SIX SLICES SHIPPED. SLICE 6 (Phase B, the correlation engine):** every feeding now contributes its whole captured protein SET to the case-crossover (`readProteinSet`, Class-A key only — the one-keying-function rule slice 5 paid for), so the hidden secondary B-416 surfaced in 34 live rows finally reaches the engine; free-fed bowls exclude EVERY protein they carry, a medication vehicle drops its WHOLE set, and a row with no set degrades byte-identically to `[primary_protein]` (proved by test). Attribution stays honest via **collinearity clustering**: proteins whose exposure indicator vectors over a symptom's matched set are EXACTLY IDENTICAL become one joint candidate, named together and credited to none — which **answers §13's open near-collinearity question with exact identity, no tunable parameter** (every statistic ① computes is a pure function of that vector, so identical vectors are bit-identically untestable; a fraction would merge proteins the data CAN separate). `CorrelationFinding.protein` became the joint LABEL rather than a representative member — safe degradation: an un-updated reader can only name both without the caveat, where a representative would have credited one and exonerated its twin by omission. Home renders a linked pair (never truncated); joint findings are template-only; Patterns' Top-Protein counts sets so the dashboard and engine cannot disagree. **The `adversarial-reviewer` FAILED it six times and every finding was a reproduced program output; all six are fixed and mutation-verified.** Highest severity: **the joint card told diet-trial owners to break their trial** — no `dietTrialActive` gate while `priorityBand` PROMOTES correlations to the lead slot for trial pets, i.e. the most dangerous copy reached the population §7 #6 says joint candidates are most likely for; fixed with an engine-resolved `jointGuidance` that swaps the action for a route to the vet without ever suppressing the finding. Then **two paths promoted an UNRELATED finding Early→Established** with byte-identical statistics (a collinearity merge shrinking the family 4→3; a free-fed bowl declaring a second protein — sharper, because the family is shared across symptom lanes while the `standingConfounder` cap is not) → fixed with a family FLOOR that can never be smaller than a primary-only engine would have made it, which keeps §7 #2 intact (a 4-protein bag still costs one comparison). **That floor is PROVISIONAL and wants a PM/Data ruling** — the unfloored numbers are arguably the more correct count of comparisons; the conservative side was taken because the alternative makes a finding's tier a function of capture events about OTHER foods, on the tier that gates the vet report. Also fixed: **joint candidates could reach `established`**, contradicting §7 #4 (now capped at Early, so a joint candidate never reaches the vet report — Dr. Chen ratification wanted); **staple-washout named one of several TIED staples**, implying the unnamed one was assessed and cleared; the attribution floor ignored the control arm; and **the "it self-resolves" argument was false as stated** (1:1 matching means most days never enter the matched set, so an owner can feed the proteins apart and see nothing — copy softened, → B-469). That did NOT weaken the exact-identity decision, which rests on the statistic being a pure function of the vector. No schema, no migration. Follow-ups: **B-467** (`ask/tools.ts` + `summary.ts` still primary-only — docstrings corrected in place, behaviour deferred because `topMealProtein` feeds a clinically-reviewed claim), **B-468** (joint candidates also fire for two always-co-fed SINGLE-protein foods — defensible but §7 #6 predicted a rare card; a dogfood frequency question), **B-469** (matched-set coverage; real fix is B-049's 1:M matching), **B-470** (set membership is binary so protein DOSE is invisible, and slice 6 makes that configuration the norm — error direction is toward the novel protein). **DEPLOY:** `generate-signal` redeploy required after merge (client renderer ships in the same PR, so the gate is merge, not a PR chain). PHASE A COMPLETE 2026-07-25 (slices 0–5; slice 5 = PR #448) and the B-416 re-derivation backfill — which now matters more than it did, because all 59 live rows still have `|proteins| ≤ 1`, so on today's data nearly every food renders single-protein-and-incomplete on the vet report. SLICE 5 (vet-report render, #448):** `generate-report`'s food join widened to `proteins, ingredients_notes, ai_extraction_confidence` and the report now renders the captured SET — the headline qualifies "day N of M" when off-trial protein reached the pet, page 1 carries the trial food's own contamination + any ad-lib bowl exposure + the provenance caveat, appendix B gains a "Proteins in the diet" block (primary bold, secondaries subordinate, provenance once), appendices C/E carry the full set, and the tally + weekly chart move to SET MEMBERSHIP (a duck-and-chicken treat is a chicken exposure; feedings and exposures are carried as separate units and the render says so). Every claim about what is NOT in a food goes through the shared D10 gate, extended to the aggregate as a "floor, not a total" disclosure. Pure predicates extracted to `lib/trialProtein.ts` so the Deno function and the client run ONE off-trial implementation (B-417 §5.3's failure mode). **The `adversarial-reviewer` FAILED it and was right:** the SET was keyed with `deriveProteinSet` (Class B) while the TARGET resolved through `canonicalizeProtein` (Class A), so a protein could fail to match ITSELF — an `ocean whitefish` trial food (3 such rows live) rendered "The trial food's own label also lists Whitefish" in bold on page 1, and the client would have said clean for the same row. `lib/protein.ts:202` had banned exactly this in writing. Fixed with **`readProteinSet`** (Class-A only); the rule to carry into slice 6 is that a READ PATH USES ONE KEYING FUNCTION. **The `vet-report-cold-read` returned NOT READY twice-run:** its best finding was not a bug — page 1 told the WRONG STORY (a free-fed chicken/turkey bowl meant the elimination diet was never run, and that fact was three pages away), and appendix E still rendered the contaminated trial food as clean `duck`. Both fixed; the re-read confirmed them resolved. Deferred + recorded: **B-488** (a stale `ai_extraction_confidence` can manufacture "nothing else on the label" after an owner edit — unsafe direction, mirror of B-437; not fixed here because the only available proxy over-triggers, so the precise fix is on the write path), **B-489/B-490** (the cold read's two pre-existing blockers; filed as B-442/B-443, renumbered on ID collisions), **B-444/B-445/B-446/B-447/B-448**. SLICE 4 (Tier-1 disclosure + the Tier-2 trial-contaminant flag):** the deterministic wedge win — §3's "highest-value slice is deterministic, not statistical" — now ships. **Tier 1 (D7):** every surface that shows a protein set states its provenance, gated on the new shared D10 predicate (`proteinSetCompleteness`, `lib/protein.ts`) — the Foods-tab library row and the capture-confirm step render `Duck · also contains chicken` / `Duck · nothing else on the label` / `Duck · ingredient list not read`; the two edit surfaces carry a provenance line under the D8 picker (the chips already show the set, so the only thing left to disclose is whether it is the WHOLE set). **Tier 2 (D2/§8):** `lib/trialContaminant.ts` — the deterministic off-trial-protein check, surfaced in three registers: a **soft confirm** at add-to-library (`TrialContaminantSheet`, "Not now / Add anyway"), a **passive, non-blocking line** on the meal completion card at log time (Principle 1 — the meal is already saved; the card carries no new tap and its dwell extends 5s→7s so the prose is readable), and a **standing note** on the food's detail screen + the diet-trial card (`TrialContaminantNote`). **Four rules keep it honest, and three of them are rulings rather than taste:** (1) **presence-only** — an empty or unread protein set yields silence, never an all-clear (D10); there is no negative form of any string in the module, by construction. (2) **B-417 C2, PM-ratified** — the trial diet's OWN contamination is a trial-level standing fact and is EXCLUDED from the per-meal path by construction; a per-feeding flag on the prescribed food would fire 100+ times across a 56-day trial. (3) **one heads-up per food per trial** — a non-trial food flags the FIRST time it is fed inside the window and stays quiet after (C2's alarm-fatigue reasoning generalized; the standing note keeps the fact visible). (4) **the trial target comes from the trial food's owner-designated `primary_protein`, never `proteins[0]`** — slice 3 writes a NULL primary when the owner clears the main and demotes it into the tail, so reading `proteins[0]` there would resurrect a cleared designation and INVERT the check, reporting the trial's own protein as the contaminant. **B-411 was read before the predicate was written, as instructed, and its gap does not open a hole here:** the check is "everything but the target", so `poultry` and `chicken fat` still surface (they are not the target key) — the under-claim costs precision in the copy, never a missed flag. **One deliberate deviation from the PM-reviewed mock, flagged for ratification:** the add-time sheet says "trial diet" where the mock said "elimination trial" — `diet_trials` carries no indication column (D6 deferred it), so asserting a trial TYPE we were never told is a fabricated clinical detail. Client plumbing: `food_items_cache` gained `ingredients_notes` + `ai_extraction_confidence` (the two D10 arms) via the existing ALTER-upgrade pattern, mirrored by `refreshFoodCache` and selected by `LIBRARY_FOODS_QUERY`. No migration, no Edge Function change. `diet_trials` has no local mirror (Supabase-only, same posture as `useTrend` / the profile card / `widgetSnapshot`), so the trial row is a TTL-cached best-effort read and offline degrades to silence. Deferred + recorded: B-437 (provenance column), B-438 (reconcile with B-417 PR 5's `lib/dietTrial.ts`), B-439 (no protein line on the quick-log tile — a Principle-1 call, not an omission). **PR 3 (manual capture, #446):** the two-line D8 picker — `components/food/ProteinSetPicker.tsx` ("Main protein" = the shipped B-332 single-select picker, `proteins[0]`; "Also contains" = multi-select secondaries over a new `components/ui/MultiChipGroup`), auto-demote on main-change **and** on clear (§6 + §11 — no captured exposure is ever lost), never-in-both enforced structurally by omitting the main from the secondaries' options, D9 on both typed escapes, and `seedPickerProteins`/`pickerProteinsToSet` in `lib/protein.ts` carrying the §11 seed rule. Both screens write `primary_protein` + `proteins` together on every save, gated on an explicit touch signal so an untouched picker still never clobbers an AI-hydrated set. **The serializer is Class-A only on purpose (D3a)** — normalizing a *seeded* value the owner never typed would be a retroactive Class-B merge; that is B-416's PM-gated question, and a test now locks the boundary. Closes **B-412** (both halves). No schema; tsc + jest 1764 (112 suites); CI green. **The `adversarial-reviewer` FAILED this slice twice before it passed** — five defects, all at the `ProteinPicker`↔`ProteinSetPicker` seam: per-keystroke emissions hit the auto-demote and wrote fabricated keys (`["bison","biso","bis","bi","b"]`) into the correlation column, a single paste of `buffalo` persisted `["bison","buffalo"]` (re-opening B-412 *inside one row*), a blur with no keystroke re-keyed a seeded `ocean whitefish` (Class-B breach), clearing the main silently undid itself on reopen, and the first fix over-corrected into dropping a retyped/backspaced custom main — i.e. exactly the novel proteins a trial is built on. All fixed + **property-tested** (no interaction sequence may invent a key; a no-op is a save→reseed fixpoint), mutation-verified. Two residuals for the PM: `primary_protein === proteins[0]` now has one deliberate exception (a cleared main writes NULL — needs a D4/039 spec nod), and **B-436** (a protein typed into "Also contains" and never blurred before Save may be dropped). **PR 1 (schema, #424):** migration 039 `food_items.proteins TEXT[]` applied live (backfill parity-verified on all 56 rows) + `food_items_cache` mirror + `refreshFoodCache` + pre-sync carriage + encoding helpers. **PR 2 (extraction, #429):** `extract-food-from-photo` emits the ordered `proteins` set + its own confidence field; `primary_protein` is the DERIVED `proteins[0]` (model primary hoisted to [0] so panel order can't demote a trial's target protein); B-048 canonicalization absorbed as a WRITE-PATH-ONLY normalizer in `lib/protein.ts` (read-time `canonicalizeProtein` deliberately un-widened per D3); set deduped/ordered/capped at 8; **deployed live as v14**. Residual → B-406. Slices 4 (disclosure+contaminant) / 5 (vet report) next — parallelizable, disjoint files, and both must read D10/B-413 before rendering a set; Phase B gated.** Spec-ready 2026-07-19 (#416): PM RATIFIED D1–D8; spec v1.0 build-ready (`docs/nyx-multi-protein-requirements.md` + mocks `docs/nyx-multi-protein-mockups.html`). Sensitivity-vs-attribution resolved as a false binary (capture wins *exposure*; collinearity-aware joint attribution keeps *culprit-ID* honest — "let the vet piece it together"). Rulings: D1 capture-all/phased; D2 contaminant flag at **add (soft) + log (non-blocking, Principle 1)**, pulled to Phase A; D3 absorb B-048; D4 `food_items.proteins TEXT[]` (`[0]`=derived primary, additive); D5 joint-candidate compute-always / Home-above-floor / vet-report-is-synthesis; D6 no `diet_trials.excluded_proteins` v1; D7 two tiers — disclosure-for-all vs. escalation-in-trial; D8 two-line "Main protein" + "Also contains" picker (auto-demote); VR vet-report primary+secondary (Dr. Chen conditions), Phase-A fast-follow. **6-PR plan (§11)** — Phase A: schema → extraction → picker → disclosure+contaminant → report; Phase B: set-membership correlation (`adversarial-reviewer` MANDATORY, deploy-gated per B-182). Absorbs B-048; interacts with B-332 (done) / B-070 / B-156 / B-040. |

PM confirmed the consequence 2026-07-16: sign in with a *different* account and you see every other account's foods in your picker (`refreshFoodCache` selects all `food_items` with no user filter; sign-out wipes the cache so each account re-hydrates the same global set). **Trust & Safety frames this as a pre-launch blocker, not just UX:** a global catalog exposes each user's added foods (their pet's actual diet — health-adjacent) plus `created_by_user_id` cross-tenant, which a privacy / App-Store review would flag; Designer/Jordan/Sam add that a stranger's foods (and typos) in your picker fail the 10-second test; Data Scientist notes per-account isolation also removes cross-user metadata contamination of correlations and makes dedup within-account (cleaner n). Ripple: reshapes the dedup track (B-009/B-018 assume a global catalog + cross-user dedup) and makes **B-005's per-user archive fall out for free** (a per-account `archived_at` IS per-user — so B-354 should precede or bundle B-005 to avoid building a throwaway per-user-archive join table). | B-358 | B-354 PR 2 — reorder `food-capture` to insert-then-upload, then owner-scope the `nyx-food-photos` Storage INSERT | Build-time discovery in B-354 PR 1 (#374): the client uploads food photos to `{foodId}/…` **before** it inserts the owner-locked `food_items` row (`app/food-capture.tsx runUploadAndExtract`), so an ownership-subquery INSERT policy would break **every** upload (42501), not just cross-tenant ones — PR 1 therefore scoped only the Storage SELECT (the launch blocker: cross-tenant *read* of health-adjacent label photos) and left INSERT authenticated+bucket-scoped. **PM decision 2026-07-16: Option A — reorder the app.** Residual gap while open is bounded: an attacker who retains a pre-migration food UUID can *plant* a new object under that prefix but **cannot read it back** (SELECT scoped), **cannot overwrite/delete** legit objects (no UPDATE/DELETE policy), and foodIds are unguessable UUIDs — `rls-privacy-reviewer` concurred it doesn't block PR 1. Fold into B-354 PR 2 (the FR-5 client cache filter + one-time truncate): (1) insert the pending `food_items` row **before** uploading its photos; (2) then add the owner-scoped INSERT policy `(storage.foldername(name))[1] IN (SELECT id::text FROM food_items WHERE created_by_user_id = auth.uid())` (mirror of the shipped SELECT). `rls-privacy-reviewer` on the tightened INSERT. | Now | 2026-07-16 | B-354 PR 2 cache half (#377, shipped); first App Store submission | **Done — 2026-07-18 (PR #392).** Reordered `app/food-capture.tsx runUploadAndExtract` to insert-then-upload; new migration `036_nyx_food_photos_owner_insert.sql` tightens the `nyx-food-photos` Storage INSERT to the owner subquery (mirror of the `033` SELECT). `rls-privacy-reviewer` **PASS** (cross-user plant / crafted-path / insert-error reorder window / service-role bypass all held); `code-reviewer` fix-before-merge findings addressed (insert-error observability split by code; deploy-order + schema-isolation justification stated in the PR). **DEPLOY-GATED:** migration 036 applied live only AFTER the reordered client reaches devices (old upload-then-insert clients would 42501). PM checks: `nyx-food-photos` bucket must be private (dashboard); optional one-time audit for pre-036 planted objects. Residual orphan-row nit → B-369. |

| B-360 | `assembleContext` swallows Supabase read errors in both analysis functions | `code-reviewer` (B-247 PR 3, 2026-07-17). In `analyze-vomit/index.ts` and `analyze-stool/index.ts`, the three `Promise.all`-ed context reads (recent vomit/loose-stool/lethargy/vomiting events + meals) never check `.error` — a failed query silently falls through to `?? []` and yields empty contextual flags, so a real repeated-diarrhea / concurrent-vomiting / feline-reduced-intake pattern could go undetected with no log trail (contrast `recordUsage`, which `console.warn`s on RPC error). CLAUDE.md's "no silent failures in sync or API calls" rule applies. **Inherited pattern, NOT introduced by PR 3** — analyze-stool deliberately mirrors the shipped vomit shape (PR 2's byte-parity goal). Safe-direction note: a swallowed read under-fires a contextual flag (never a false escalation, never a false reassurance — the read still lands as monitor/not_enough_to_say), so it degrades toward under-detection, not toward a reassuring verdict. Fix: surface `.error` (at least `console.warn`, ideally treat a context-read failure as a soft escalation-preserving signal) in the shared-ish `assembleContext` of both functions. | Later | 2026-07-17 | Per-incident contextual-flag reliability | Open — surfaced 2026-07-17 (code-reviewer, B-247 PR 3). |
| B-361 | Contextual-window anchor mismatch (`now` vs `occurred_at`) misses repeats on back-dated events | `adversarial-reviewer` (B-247 PR 3, finding ③, 2026-07-17). In both `analyze-vomit` and `analyze-stool`, `assembleContext` FETCHES candidate events with a SQL floor anchored to `now` (`occurred_at >= now-24h`), but `computeContextualFlags` COUNTS them with `within()` anchored to the analysed event's `occurred_at`. For a witnessed event logged live (`occurred_at ≈ now`) they coincide, but for a **back-dated / discovered** event (`occurred_at` well before `now`) the SQL floor can drop a prior in-window event that `within()` would have counted → `repeated_loose_stool` / `repeated_vomiting` under-fires. `concurrent_vomiting`/`concurrent_lethargy` have the same now-vs-event anchor. Under-escalation only (never a false escalation, never a false reassurance), bounded to retroactively-dated events; **inherited from shipped vomit, not introduced by PR 3.** Fix consistently in both functions: anchor the fetch window to `thisEventOccurredAt` (widen the SQL floor to `occurred_at - windowHours`), or fetch a wider band and let `within()` do the gating. Pairs with the same Dr. Chen pass as the B-247 seam. | Later | 2026-07-17 | Per-incident contextual-flag reliability (vomit + stool) | Open — surfaced 2026-07-17 (adversarial-reviewer, B-247 PR 3 ③). |
| B-362 | Stool `monitor` read + "Keep an eye out" banner misfire on a NORMAL stool | `pm-feature-review` (B-247 PR 6, 2026-07-17), highest-value catch. A clear normal (Bristol Type 3/4) stool has no flag → the floor returns `monitor` → the banner reads "Keep an eye out" and `buildMonitorReadText` (`analyze-stool/index.ts`) says "…if it happens again, or {pet} seems unwell or goes off food, your vet is the best call." Written when the only incident type was ALWAYS-abnormal vomit; stool spans healthy→watery, so on the diet-trial owner's happy path (logging a *good* stool to track progress) it frames a healthy poop as a symptom. It's the never-reassure invariant working correctly — but the *wording* treats every stool as a problem, and this is the most-seen stool read (most stools are normal) → "why is it warning me about a normal poop?" trust erosion. **Not this renderer's copy — it's the PR-3 Edge Function template** (a shipped clinical function; any change needs its own `adversarial-reviewer` pass + Dr. Chen). Needs a PM decision: a stool-specific monitor template + possibly a softer header than "Keep an eye out" for Type 3/4, that neither reassures nor implies a normal stool recurring is concerning. | Now | 2026-07-17 | Wide rollout of the stool read; Dr. Chen + nyx-voice + PM copy call | **Done — 2026-07-17 (B-247 PR 4, #390).** PM-delegated at kickoff; **team ruling = fix the body copy, keep the header.** `buildMonitorReadText` (`analyze-stool/index.ts`) reworked from the vomit-era `"…if it happens again…"` (which framed a *normal* stool as a symptom to fear recurring) to a **state-silent, trend-building** read: *"One stool on its own is just a single snapshot — it can't show how {pet}'s gut is doing over time. That fuller picture comes from logging the next few. If {pet} keeps having loose stools, seems unwell, or goes off food, your vet is the best call."* Reframes n=1 as a data point on the diet-trial wedge (logging a good stool is the *right* action), never asserts wellness. **Header `"Keep an eye out"` kept** — clinical-guardrails Pattern 1 + nyx-voice Pattern 6 name it as the sanctioned forward-looking monitor label; a blanket change would mis-serve the single-loose case that shares the `monitor` bucket (consistency-aware header = optional client follow-up, not built). **Gates:** `adversarial-reviewer` **2-round PASS** — round 1 flagged an Axis-2 gap (dropping the recurrence cue narrowed the single-loose-stool net for a non-logging owner); round 2 confirmed the restored state-silent persistence cue closes it with no reassurance-by-implication leak; Dr. Chen/clinical-guardrails lens applied (escalation floor untouched, mucus structured-field-only, consistent with the ratified B-247 seam). Deno suite green (59); rode into the PR-3 function pre-deploy. Consistency-aware header + the client `StoolAnalysisSection` `REC_LABEL` stay as shipped. |

Counterexample (c) — the declining-intake cat asked "what does she prefer?" — holds at the copy layer (the "picky"/"fussy"/"finicky"/preference-masking bans in the validator + system prompt), but the STRUCTURAL gap is that Ask cannot *see* a decline: `intake_summary` returns a single-window rate and `symptom_trend` only accepts `ASK_SYMPTOM_TYPES` (not intake), so a *falling* finished-rate can't be surfaced, and the safety register can't be routed to it. Today the only escalation path is an engine relay (§7.2), which itself needs logged meals to fire — so a low-logging decline is doubly invisible. Fix = an `intake_trend` tool (window-vs-prior finished-rate delta) that reuses detector ④'s `isWorsening`-style predicate so Ask and the Signal can't disagree (G5/G4), routed to the calm health register on a decline. `adversarial-reviewer` mandatory when built. | Next | 2026-07-18 | Ask intake-decline safety; general-mode flip-on (§7.5) | Open — surfaced 2026-07-18 (adversarial pass, A7). |
| B-383 | Ask numeral-subset can launder a mis-attributed count (window/date integers in the allowed set) | `adversarial-reviewer` (Ask A7, 2026-07-18). `validateAnswer`'s numeral-subset (`collectNumerals`) harvests numerals from tool-result STRINGS too — the window label ("the last **7** days"), denominators, and ISO dates ("2026-07-**09**") — so a fabricated/mis-attributed count that happens to equal one of those integers ("vomited **7** times" when the true count is 0, `7` sourced from the window) passes the gate. Origin-bug class (existence, not attribution). Server-built provenance shows the TRUE denominator (a contradicting mitigation), but the Newsreader headline — the most-read line — carries the mis-attributed number unguarded. Fix = per-claim validation (bind each numeral to the specific tool field it's meant to render, not the union), or at minimum keep window/date integers OUT of the count-validation set. Also fold in `sanitizeFollowups` numeral screening (a follow-up chip "Why did she vomit 5 times?" asserts an unverified count). | Next | 2026-07-18 | Ask answer integrity (G5/AC-9) | Open — surfaced 2026-07-18 (adversarial pass, A7). |

A7 closed the "percent"/"per cent" hole (any percent word is a computed figure the tools never return), but spelled fractions ("three-quarters") and count words ("vomited **three** times", "**twice**") still bypass the no-arithmetic rule. Left narrow on purpose — "twice a day" is a legitimate med frequency (`frequencyLabel`), so a blanket ban would false-positive real answers; needs a context-aware number-word parser, not a bigger regex. Low severity (the prompt instructs digits + "N of M"; the digit path is already gated). | Later | 2026-07-18 | Ask answer integrity tail | Open — surfaced 2026-07-18 (adversarial pass, A7). |
| B-385 | Ask's product-wide never-reassure gate is a denylist (open-vocabulary implication-reassurance slips it) | `adversarial-reviewer` (Ask A8, round-3/4, 2026-07-18). The A8 photo-read channel is structurally closed (the model is handed NO read info + a categorical photo-reference bar + the deterministic `buildReadLine`), but the *general* Ask answer path still relies on `REASSURANCE_RE` — a denylist — for the model's own prose, and open-vocabulary implication-reassurance with no bannable token ("that one's textbook", "par for the course", "uneventful", "run of the mill") slips it. This is NOT A8-specific: it applies to every model-phrased Ask answer (even a count), which is the fundamental "model phrases → denylist gates" tension. The structural fixes exist only where the datum is clinical enough to warrant them (the photo read, the analyze-vomit monitor text). A fuller close = move the general gate from a word-denylist to an allowlisted-recount shape, or an LLM-judge veto (composes with the open "gestalt reviewer" LLM-ladder question). On a "compare the last two" turn (a no-flag read A then a present-flag read B — the exact case the 2-per-message live-read budget enables), the most-recent B is an escalation, so the bar is OFF, and a residual-#3-class reassurance about A ("the first one was uneventful") could slip. **Structurally contained, not a clean break** (A was also redacted → no factual hook; `buildReadLine` still names B's flag deterministically → escalation never suppressed), so it's low-severity. | B-412 | Owner-typed "Other" protein bypasses the extraction normalizer — one animal, two keys | **Found by the `adversarial-reviewer` on B-351 PR 2 (#429); live now.** Extraction applies `normalizeExtractedProtein` at write time, so an AI read of "Buffalo" stores `bison`; the picker's **"Other" typed escape** stores raw and is only `canonicalizeProtein`'d on read, so an owner typing "Buffalo" stores `buffalo`. Same animal, two keys — exposure splits across them, each falls under the effective-n floor and neither surfaces, and the vet report lists both as separate exposures. This is the B-052 fragmentation `lib/protein.ts` exists to prevent, reintroduced through the second capture route. Affected terms are only the aliased/stripped ones (buffalo·deer·ocean whitefish·egg product·tissue and descriptor forms); **every COMMON_PROTEINS chip is unaffected, locked by a test.** Fixing it is a PRODUCT decision, not a silent normalizer change — *does the app rewrite what the owner typed?* Options: (a) run the write-path normalizer on the Other value too (consistent keying; the owner sees their typed "Buffalo" become "Bison"), (b) normalize for the correlation key but display what they typed (needs a second column), (c) drop the alias table and accept vaguer keys. **RULED 2026-07-24 (PM) — option (d): normalize on write, and show the owner why** (spec §10 **D9**). The typed escape is a WRITE path, so `normalizeExtractedProtein` is in-contract (D3a). The rewrite must be legible: resolve **on commit, never per keystroke**; the matching chip selects so the change is visible in the control; a quiet persistent inline note explains it (*"Saved as Bison — that's the label name for buffalo."*). Toast/`Snackbar` considered and rejected — it narrates a vanished event and leaves an unexplained `Bison` behind. `nyx-voice` pass on the note. Also in scope: `app/food/[id].tsx` + `app/food-capture.tsx` write `primary_protein` **without** `proteins`, so an owner correcting a food today desyncs the pair — and PR 3's picker, reseeding from the stored `proteins` array (§6), would silently REVERT that correction on screen. PR 3 needs an explicit reconciliation rule for rows written in this window. | Now | 2026-07-24 | **B-351 PR 3 (the D8 picker) — blocking; PR 3 owns this surface** | **Done — 2026-07-25 (PR #446); REOPENED AND RE-CLOSED 2026-07-26 (PR #462).** The build-35 QA found D9 never fired in practice: it commits on blur, and both host screens use `keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled"`, where tapping Save does not blur the field — so `buffalo` saved raw, and (worse) the outgoing main was never demoted, silently dropping a captured protein. Fixed with an imperative `commitPending()` the hosts call at the save boundary. The lesson worth keeping: every test called `fireEvent(field,'blur')` directly, so the suite verified the commit handler and never that a real Save reached it. Original close text follows. **Done — 2026-07-25 (PR #446).** D9 shipped as ruled, in `components/food/ProteinPicker.tsx` + the new `ProteinSetPicker.tsx`, sharing one note module (`components/food/proteinNote.tsx`) so both typed escapes read identically. Resolves on **commit** (blur/submit), never per keystroke; the matching chip selects; a persistent inline note names both values in one of two registers — *"that's the label name for buffalo"* (swapped) / *"that's the protein in chicken liver"* (kept). Silent when only casing changed, and text the normalizer can't use is left as typed rather than wiped. **The second half — the primary/`proteins` desync window — is closed too:** both `app/food-capture.tsx` and `app/food/[id].tsx` now write both columns together on every save (`primary_protein = proteins[0]`), server row and SQLite mirror, and PR 3's `seedPickerProteins` carries the §11 seed rule for rows written in the window (owner's primary wins; clearing the main demotes rather than drops). `nyx-voice` ✓ · `adversarial-reviewer` run at wrap. |
| B-413 | An empty `proteins` set can't distinguish "panel unread" from "no secondaries" | **Found by the `adversarial-reviewer` on B-351 PR 2 (#429).** The prompt says an illegible panel yields `[]`, but `primary_protein`'s description invites reading the front of pack — so a marketing-name-only read produces `proteins=['duck']`, which is **indistinguishable in the column** from a set actually read off the panel. Under Tier-1 disclosure that renders as "Duck · nothing else", and under the vet report's provenance line ("Proteins as read from product labels") it reads as a clean single-protein food. **Absence of secondaries here means "panel unread", not "clean"** — reassurance-on-absence, the `clinical-guardrails` asymmetry, on the surface a vet trusts most. The truth is in `ai_extraction_confidence.proteins` (0 when unread); the `proteins` column alone cannot carry it. PR 4 (disclosure) and PR 5 (vet report) must GATE their rendering on that confidence value — show "ingredients not captured" rather than an implied-complete set. Not yet rendered anywhere, so this is a forward hazard, not a live defect. **RULED 2026-07-24 (PM) — option (a): a derived completeness predicate, no schema** (spec §10 **D10**). The gate is the CONJUNCTION of panel-text captured AND `ai_extraction_confidence.proteins` above a floor — a model's self-reported confidence is the least trustworthy field to gate on alone, and the set has four provenances (manual · failed/capped · front-of-pack-only · panel-read), not two. An explicit provenance column stays named as the D4a upgrade if the derived predicate proves too coarse. Slice 5 must widen `generate-report`'s food join (`proteins, ai_extraction_confidence, ingredients_notes`). | Now | 2026-07-24 | **B-351 PR 4 (disclosure) + PR 5 (vet report) — read before rendering a protein set** | Partial — 2026-07-25 (slice 4). The gate is BUILT and is now the single shared implementation: `proteinSetCompleteness` / `hasCapturedPanelText` / `proteinReadConfidence` in `lib/protein.ts` (the dual-import client/Deno module, so slice 5's `generate-report` uses the SAME predicate rather than a second one that could disagree), floors `MIN_PANEL_TEXT_LENGTH` 12 + `MIN_PROTEIN_READ_CONFIDENCE` 0.5, conjunction as ruled. Every slice-4 surface is gated on it: the library row and capture-confirm summary say `ingredient list not read` instead of `nothing else on the label`, the under-picker line always states provenance, and the diet-trial card says the trial food's panel was never read rather than leaving the absence of a flag to read as an all-clear. Client plumbing: `food_items_cache` gained `ingredients_notes` + `ai_extraction_confidence` (ALTER-upgrade pattern), mirrored by `refreshFoodCache`, selected by `LIBRARY_FOODS_QUERY`. One floor found in test, not review: `Ingredients:` is itself exactly 12 characters, so the bare heading cleared the raw length floor — `hasCapturedPanelText` now strips a leading panel label before measuring. **Remaining: slice 5** — widen `generate-report`'s food join to `proteins, ai_extraction_confidence, ingredients_notes` and gate the report render on the same predicate. Provenance-column upgrade tracked as B-437. |
| B-414 | `canonicalizeProtein` is not idempotent when a qualifier strip exposes punctuation | **Found by the `adversarial-reviewer` on B-351 PR 2 (#429); pre-existing B-052 defect, not introduced by that PR.** `C('chicken - meal')` → `'chicken -'`, and `C('chicken -')` → `'chicken'` — the punctuation trim runs once, before the looped qualifier strip, so a value can still re-key on a second pass. The module's own docstring claims idempotence. The extraction write path is immune (its fixpoint loop re-canonicalizes), but **migration 039's one-shot SQL mirror reproduces the same shape**, so a legacy row like "Chicken - Meal" would have backfilled to `{'chicken -'}`. **Verified clean on live data (0 of 56 rows affected)** — latent, not live corruption. **RULED 2026-07-24 (PM) — fix now, not Later, and the governing rule was revised with it.** The PM's read: *"same word and punctuation artifact.. we need to catch that and allow that to be merged so that we have great data. Garbage data in garbage data out."* The original deferral leaned on D3 ("never re-merge already-stored keys") — but D3 exists to guard *semantic* merges, where a wrong call pools two species invisibly. A stray hyphen is not a judgement call, and the merge risk was empirically **zero** (0 of 59 live rows), so the change is behaviour-neutral today and strictly correct going forward. Now **D3a** (spec §10): Class A (orthographic/artifact) merges are permitted on read, retroactively; Class B (semantic) stays write-path-only. Fixed: joint punctuation+qualifier fixpoint · hyphen-joined qualifiers · the length guard · a cross-product **convergence property test** (the old four-item example list passed for months while the bug was live — that is the counter-force the ruling actually asked for). Both follow-ups are DONE: (1) the punctuation trim now loops with the qualifier strip to a joint fixpoint; (2) the length guard landed at `canonicalizeProtein`'s entry — the shared path B-416's backfill will enter through — since the normalizer's fixpoint loop is O(n²) (12 KB → 244 ms, 48 KB → 3.3 s), bounded today only by `max_tokens: 2048` on the vision call, which that backfill would not respect. | Now | 2026-07-24 | **B-416 re-derivation backfill (prerequisite)** | **Done — 2026-07-24** (shipped this session) |
| B-415 | Widget extension advertises iPad after the app went iPhone-only | **Found while verifying the B-269 `supportsTablet: false` flip (2026-07-24).** `ios.supportsTablet: false` correctly sets the **app** target to `TARGETED_DEVICE_FAMILY = "1"`, but `expo-widgets` **hardcodes** the widget extension target to `"1,2"` (`node_modules/expo-widgets/plugin/src/ios/xcode/addXCConfigurationList.ts:20`) with no config passthrough — so the extension declares iPad support the container app doesn't have. Neither `expo config --type introspect` nor any repo file can see this (it's a pbxproj build setting written at prebuild); it only appears in a real prebuild/build. Practical impact is probably nil — an extension can't install where its container app can't — but a device-family superset in an extension is a known class of App Store upload validation complaint, so it should be *checked*, not assumed. Fix if it does bite: a small `withXcodeProject` config plugin (or a `plugins/` patch alongside `withoutPushEntitlement`) pinning the `ExpoWidgetsTarget` configurations to `"1"`. **Do not pre-emptively patch** — verify at guide step 10's build-cut first. | Next | 2026-07-24 | Guide step 10 (production build-cut / built-artifact verification) | Open |
| B-436 | Typed protein can be lost or stored raw when Save wins the race with blur | **Found by the `adversarial-reviewer` on B-351 PR 3 (#446); the one probe it could not settle statically.** Both food screens use `keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled"`, so tapping Save with a typed-but-unblurred "Other" field dispatches blur AND press; whether the D9 commit's state flush lands before the save closure reads the picker state is platform- and ordering-dependent. Two shapes if the press wins. **Main line:** the raw pre-normalization value persists (`buffalo`, not `bison`) — the B-412 split, unfixed by D9 in that window. **Secondaries line (worse, and deterministic rather than a race):** that draft is LOCAL component state and emits nothing until commit, so a protein typed into "Also contains" and never blurred is **dropped entirely** — an exposure silently not captured, which is the exact failure B-351 exists to prevent, and it is invisible. Fix direction: commit at SAVE time rather than blur time (the host asks the picker to flush, or the draft is lifted into host state). Meanwhile the on-device QA script carries the step — type into Other, tap Save immediately, reopen the food. In React Native `onBlur` lands on touch-START and `onPress` on release, so the flush is expected to win in practice; that is a reasoned expectation, not a measurement. | Next | 2026-07-25 | B-351 slice 4/5 inherit the same picker; verify on-device before the extraction gate flip | Open |

That under-claims in one real case: an owner who types the full panel into `ingredients_notes` AND curates the full protein set by hand still reads as "ingredient list not read", because nothing in the row attests the second was derived from the first. Under-claiming is the safe direction (it can only suppress a completeness claim, never grant one), so this is a polish item, not a defect — but if dogfood shows the qualifier nagging on hand-curated foods, the fix is the explicit column D4a deferred, not a looser floor. Neither arm carries any signal for *did you see the whole panel*, so the module's "under-claims by construction" note is **not** true in that direction. This is inherent to the ratified conjunction rather than a slip, and it is the one way the single completeness-claiming string in the feature can still be false. Note the asymmetry the reviewer names: the **positive** branch of this same line carries cases, controls and a p-value, while the negative branch carries nothing, so *absence of a finding renders with more confidence than a finding* — the reassurance direction, on the report's most-scanned surface. | B-460 | B-416's derivation is a lexicon scan, so it finds only animals it knows — a real panel read is the only complete fix | **The named, accepted limit of the B-416 backfill (2026-07-26).** The pass scans stored `ingredients_notes` for animal-protein label phrases; a panel listing an animal outside the lexicon (kangaroo, guinea fowl on a raw grind, an exotic novel protein) yields nothing for that term. It is an **under**-capture, which is the safe direction — those foods captured *nothing* before — and it is why the pass deliberately does not write `ai_extraction_confidence`: a keyword scan cannot attest that a panel was READ, and licensing "nothing else on the label" off one would be reassurance-on-absence on the vet report (D10 / `clinical-guardrails`). Two further deliberate bounds: **plant proteins are out of scope** (the extractor emits `hydrolyzed soy protein` / `pea protein`, but adding `pea`/`soy` to 43 rows at once would rewrite every food's rendered set and the report's exposure tally on a judgement no spec asked for), and **hydrolysed terms are skipped entirely** rather than folded into their intact animal, since a hydrolysed diet is clinically a different exposure. **The real closure is re-extracting the library under the current extractor** (which reads the panel and reports its own confidence), not a bigger lexicon — a longer word list buys coverage without ever buying the attestation. Pairs with B-437 (the provenance/coverage column). | Later | 2026-07-26 | Post-dogfood; or whenever the library is re-photographed | Open |

Surfacing the source term turns a silent inference into the **Tier-1 education win D7 already names** — *"Duck · also contains chicken, from 'chicken broth'"* is the moment an owner learns hidden proteins hide in broths, meals and by-products, which is the single most useful thing a diet-trial owner can learn. **The safe shape is provenance DISPLAY, not a question.** Do NOT ask *"is this right?"*: the answer that removes a protein makes a food look cleaner, which is the unsafe direction (a food reads more trial-safe than it is), and an owner cannot adjudicate a derivation in the abstract. Showing the term lets them spot a wrong one against the bag in their hand and correct it through the **existing** slice-3 picker — same shape as B-028, where the owner edits an AI read rather than being interrogated by it. | B-462 | Disclose fats/oils and plant proteins to the vet WITHOUT routing them through the contaminant flag | **PM steer 2026-07-26: *"err on surfacing a protein for the vet… make this robust."*** B-416's lexicon was widened hard on it (novel mammals, birds, the full fish/shellfish range, insect protein, dairy; hydrolysates changed from dropped to captured-whole). Two categories were deliberately left out because widening them is **not a data decision**: `food_items.proteins` feeds the SHIPPED owner-facing contaminant flag as well as the vet report, and every key in it becomes owner copy — *"This one has X. {Pet}'s duck trial should skip X."* **(1) Fats/oils** — the exclusion is what stops `chicken fat` in Hill's DUCK entrée from firing a contaminant flag on a novel-protein trial diet. But the mainstream veterinary position (rendered chicken fat is acceptable in an elimination diet) is the **vet's** call, and D1's governing steer is *"let the vet piece it together"* — so a vet wants it disclosed while an owner told their duck trial food contains chicken fat would reasonably panic about something clinically fine. **(2) Plant proteins** (`soy`, `pea protein`, `wheat gluten`, `corn protein meal`) — real elimination-trial exposures that the extractor already emits, but nearly every dry treat in the live library carries corn or wheat, so including them makes almost **every** treat flag off-trial: a volume change to a safety prompt, i.e. an alarm-fatigue question (Principle 4), not a completeness one. **Persona conflict, recorded not resolved** — Dr. Chen: disclose both, withholding an ingredient from the report is my judgement to make. Designer/Jordan: every extra key spends the prompt's credibility, and *"should skip chicken fat"* is advice a vet would contradict. **The likely resolution is not a wider lexicon but a SECOND channel** — a vet-report disclosure line that is not a `proteins` key and therefore never reaches the contaminant check. Needs a design pass. Note dairy WAS included and is the precedent worth reading: it added 7 rows and **zero** new alarms, because every affected row already carried chicken. | Next | 2026-07-26 | B-351 slice 6 or the next vet-report pass; needs a design round | Open |

The headline cases all resolved — `Tiki Cat after DARK Rabbit & Chicken Liver` `["rabbit"]` → `["rabbit","duck","chicken"]` (a rabbit novel-protein food that would have passed the contaminant check clean), `Blue Buffalo Wilderness Duck` → `["duck","chicken","turkey"]`, `Hill's Duck & Vegetable` → `["duck","pork","turkey"]`, `Weruva Lamb Burger-ini` → `["lamb","fish","tuna"]` (sold as lamb, second ingredient tuna). **The open PM sub-decision was put and RATIFIED 2026-07-26 — yes, re-key the primaries.** Blast radius was exactly 3 rows (`ocean whitefish` → `whitefish`); every other stored primary was already normalize-stable. The argument that decided it: the mapping is ALREADY shipped on the write path (spec §5's own B-048 example, live in `EXTRACTION_PROTEIN_ALIASES`), so declining would not have held the status quo — it would have guaranteed a widening split where a food photographed tomorrow stores `whitefish` while these three keep `ocean whitefish` forever. The pass invented no species judgement of its own. **Reporting split A-vs-B, which the first run got wrong:** 10 primaries were rewritten, but only **3** are Class B — the other **7** are Class A (`Chicken By-Product Meal` → `chicken`, `Rabbit` → `rabbit`: casing and processing qualifiers, permitted always and retroactively). Collapsing them into one "re-keyed: 10" overstated what was ratified; `BackfillPlan.classBRekey` now carries the distinction separately. **Built as a pure, jest-tested module** (`lib/proteinBackfill.ts` + 33 tests, mutation-verified) rather than a re-run of the vision model: a backfill over clinical data has to be auditable row by row BEFORE it is applied, and 43 unverified model outputs is not that. `scripts/backfill-proteins.mjs` emits SQL + a before/after report + per-row rollback rather than writing directly (D3a's standing guard, satisfied by construction); all three artifacts committed under `scripts/b416-protein-backfill/`. **Both guards held, verified on live data:** zero rows where `canonicalize(primary_protein) ≠ proteins[0]`, zero rows carrying a protein twice, zero `ocean whitefish` left anywhere. Idempotence verified end-to-end — the applied database state hashes IDENTICAL to the independently-computed expected state, and a re-plan over the post-state produces zero further changes. Zero stored keys lost. The lexicon deliberately excludes fats/oils/flavours/hydrolysates, which is what stops `chicken fat` in Hill's DUCK entrée from appending `chicken` to a novel-protein trial food and firing a false contaminant flag. **Deliberately NOT written: `ai_extraction_confidence`** — a keyword scan is a presence detector, not a completeness attestation, so these rows still read "ingredient list not read" on the vet report rather than manufacturing "nothing else on the label" off a lexicon (D10 / `clinical-guardrails`; the real fix is B-437). **WIDENED SAME DAY on the PM steer *"err on surfacing a protein for the vet"*:** the first lexicon optimised the wrong direction — a MISSED protein tells a vet a contaminated food is clean (the failure B-351 exists to prevent), while an over-eager one is visible and checkable against the bag. Added novel mammals, birds, the full fish/shellfish range, insect protein and **dairy** (a first-rank allergen routinely excluded from elimination diets, live on panels as "dried cultured skim milk" / "dried cheese"); **hydrolysates changed from DROPPED to captured-whole** — the original behaviour made a hydrolysed prescription diet render with **no protein at all** to a vet, the worst possible miss, while folding it to `chicken` would claim an exposure the pet never had, so `hydrolyzed chicken` is now its own key. Two false positives the widening introduced were caught by test before they reached data: `sole` (matches "sole source of vitamin K" — dropped) and `milk` inside "milk thistle" (a botanical — now excluded). **Delta: +7 rows, multi-protein 31 → 34, and ZERO new contaminant alarms** — every affected row already carried chicken, so the flag copy just names one more protein. End state re-verified: applying both increments lands on a hash identical to one clean pass from the original. **Two categories deliberately NOT widened → B-462** (fats/oils and plant proteins; both would change shipped owner-facing contaminant copy at volume, so they are a PM/design call, not a data one). Residuals → **B-459** (the D9 disclosure gap on the 3 re-keyed rows), **B-460** (lexicon coverage — a real panel read is the only complete fix), **B-461** (show WHY a protein is in a set — the panel term; converges with D4a/B-437) and **B-462**. **Two guards written in from B-351 slice 5's adversarial re-check (2026-07-25) — this pass is where the off-trial keying bug can return as DATA rather than code.** Slice 5 fixed it by keying the report's read path Class-A only (`readProteinSet`), which holds because migration 039's backfill left `canonicalize(primary_protein) === proteins[0]` on every row. This backfill is the thing that can break that. **(1) Rewrite `primary_protein` and `proteins` ATOMICALLY in the same statement** — re-keying the array while leaving the primary verbatim recreates the exact failure (`primary='Ocean Whitefish'` + `proteins=['whitefish']` → the set becomes `['ocean whitefish','whitefish']` and page 1 announces "The record shows Whitefish reaching {pet} during the trial" for a whitefish trial; verified reproducible). **(2) Append secondaries through `normalizeExtractedProtein`, not `canonicalizeProtein`** — this is a write path, so Class B is in-contract here, and using the read-time key would start splitting `Chicken Liver` from `Chicken` as two bands in the vet report's exposure tally. |

Possible mitigations: compare `format` as well as brand+product, or fall through to rung 2 on a key-only match. Not a reason to reverse D6b — the eviction is bounded by the ~1h access-token window, so an online device flushes normally — but the underlying gap is worth closing on its own merits (a deliberate sign-out costs the same data today). | B-431 | `nyx-pet-photos` INSERT policy is `TO public` — the one *public* bucket accepts **anon** writes | Surfaced by the 2026-07-25 competitive-review platform audit and **verified live** against `pg_policies`: `pet_photos_insert` is `FOR INSERT TO public WITH CHECK (bucket_id = 'nyx-pet-photos')` — no owner scope, and the `public` role includes `anon`. `nyx-pet-photos` is also the only `public = true` bucket, and the anon key is committed in `eas.json` for all three build profiles + inlined into every client bundle. Net: anyone holding the anon key can upload arbitrary objects into a publicly-readable bucket on the project's domain (storage-cost + content-hosting abuse; not a read of anyone's pet data). Distinct from B-244/B-248 (those are cross-tenant *reads* of private buckets) and from B-358/migration 036 (that owner-scopes `nyx-food-photos` INSERT — the same fix pattern, different bucket). The 2026-07-20 hardening audit flagged public *reads* on this bucket (§A3) but not the anon *write*. Fix mirrors 036: owner-scoped `WITH CHECK` keyed on `(storage.foldername(name))[1] IN (SELECT id::text FROM pets WHERE user_id = auth.uid())`, `TO authenticated`. Closed by CHECK `pets_photo_path_pet_prefix` — free at 0 rows, expensive once photos exist. Live-verified post-apply: 3 policies, all `{authenticated}`, no `{public}`/`{anon}`; constraint validated; `get_advisors` clean (no new lint). The anon-write hole this row names is CLOSED in production. **Adjacent and still open — not slices of this row:** the on-device upload + replacement test (which settles the separate 42501 Open Question, and is the PR's merge gate) and the §A3 public-read posture (a PM call). | B-467 | The two remaining descriptive protein rankings still key off `primary_protein` alone | B-351 slice 6 moved the correlation engine (`generate-signal/detection.ts`) and the Patterns dashboard (`lib/analytics.ts` `computeTopProteins`) onto set membership, per §11's slice-6 line. **Two further copies of the same ranking were deliberately left behind and are now inconsistent with the engine:** `supabase/functions/ask/tools.ts` `topProteins` (the Ask surface's "what does she eat most" answer) and `generate-signal/summary.ts` `topMealProtein` (the AI monthly-summary clause). Both under-count exposure — a protein reaching the pet as a hidden SECONDARY is invisible to them — which is a sensitivity gap, never a false claim, so this is a consistency defect rather than a safety one. Left out of slice 6 on purpose: bundling three copy-bearing descriptive rankings into the adversarial-mandatory statistical PR would have made the falsification pass review copy churn. **`topMealProtein` is the delicate one** — its own header says the meals-only narrowing is a clinically-reviewed AI-summary claim and forbids silent "alignment", so widening it to sets needs an explicit nod, not a mechanical edit. `ask/tools.ts` is a straight mirror of `computeTopProteins` and can copy that change verbatim, including the "shares no longer sum to 1" semantics. | Next | 2026-07-26 | B-351 slice 6 (shipped); do before the Ask track's copy pass | Open |
| B-468 | Joint candidates now fire for two always-co-fed SINGLE-protein foods, not just multi-protein foods | Surfaced by B-351 slice 6's own test suite, not designed for. The collinearity guardrail clusters proteins whose exposure vectors over a symptom's matched set are identical — and that is satisfied by two separate single-protein foods fed on the same days (the 9am wet + 10am treat routine), not only by one bag declaring two proteins. The pre-existing `multi-implication` fixture flipped from two cards to one joint candidate because of it. **This follows directly from the ratified D5 guardrail and is defensible on the merits** (one indistinguishable piece of evidence stops being presented as two independent findings, the Bonferroni family stops double-counting it, and the owner gets a resolving action) — but the spec §7 #6 predicted a *rare* card for "a mono-food cat or a strict single-food trial dog", and a fixed feeding routine is neither rare nor unusual. So the real open question is **frequency**: if joint cards turn out to be common on real diets, the Home surface may be showing "we can't tell these apart" more often than it shows an attributable culprit, which is honest but not necessarily useful. Watch it in dogfood before deciding anything; the fix, if one is needed, is a surfacing rule (D5 already says Home shows a joint candidate only above the single-culprit floor), never a loosening of the clustering. | Next | 2026-07-26 | Dogfood observation post-slice-6 deploy | Open — PM nod wanted on the behaviour change; no code action yet |
| B-469 | The joint card's resolving action can silently do nothing (matched-set coverage) | Reproduced by the B-351 slice-6 adversarial pass. The joint card tells the owner "feeding one without the other would start to separate them", and separation only registers if the divergent day is selected by the 1:1 nearest-eligible-control matcher as a case or control window. Most days never enter the matched set, so an owner can do exactly what the card asks and see the card unchanged on the next regeneration — with nothing anywhere telling them the attempt didn't count. Verified: separating the proteins on day 0 (never a chosen control) left the cluster intact; the same separation on day 1 (a chosen control) split it. **Not a slice-6 defect** — it is a property of 1:1 matching, whose named fix is **B-049's 1:M conditional matching** (already on the books), and slice 6's copy was softened from "would tell them apart" to "would start to separate them" so it no longer promises a result the engine may not deliver. Worth doing something better eventually: either 1:M matching, or surfacing how many separated feedings have actually reached the analysis so the action has visible progress. | Later | 2026-07-26 | B-049 (1:M matching) | Open |
| B-470 | Set-membership exposure is binary, so protein DOSE is invisible — and slice 6 makes that the norm | Surfaced by the B-351 slice-6 adversarial pass as a residual on §7 #1, and it bites the wedge specifically. A window is "exposed" to a protein if ANY in-window feeding carries it, so two chicken-bearing feedings and one are the same observation. In the reviewer's own §7 #1 fixture the case windows carried **two** chicken-bearing feedings and the controls **one** — chicken co-varied in dose with duck — and the engine attributed the entire difference to duck. Direction of the error: false attribution to the novel protein plus exoneration-by-omission of the staple, on the elimination-trial surface. **Slice 6 makes this configuration ordinary rather than exotic**: adding chicken to the novel-protein bag is precisely what raises staple dose on trial days, which is the whole scenario B-351 exists to detect. Binary membership was the right v1 (it is what makes a staple wash out cleanly, and a dose model needs portion data the app does not capture), so this is a real limitation to design against, not a bug to patch — probably a count-weighted or dose-aware exposure once portions exist, and it must not break the concordance washout that binary membership buys. `adversarial-reviewer` mandatory if built. | Later | 2026-07-26 | Portion/serving-size capture; Step 10 correlation quality | Open |
| B-471 | Edge-Function deploys require the agent to retype the whole bundle — provision a Supabase PAT as a cloud-env secret | The MCP `deploy_edge_function` tool takes the function body as **inline text**, so the only deploy path in this environment is for the agent to read the built bundle into its own context and re-emit it verbatim. `generate-signal` is **108 KB / 2,457 lines** — too large for one contiguous read, so it arrives across six tool results and has to be reassembled by hand. A silent truncation ships a broken clinical Edge Function to production. It *is* detectable (the runbook already mandates a sha256 read-back + a boot smoke test) and recoverable by redeploying, and prior sessions have done it successfully — but the risk is unnecessary and it scales the wrong way: `generate-report` and `ask` are the same size or larger, and every one of these deploys is now the most fragile step in an otherwise well-gated pipeline. **This blocked the B-351 slice 6 redeploy (2026-07-26), which is why it is filed rather than theorised.** Fix: mint a Supabase personal access token and set it as a **cloud-env secret** — never in the repo, exactly as the Secrets Register already anticipates ("only needed if the PM later wants `supabase functions deploy` straight from disk, in which case set it as a cloud-env secret, never in the repo"). Then `npx supabase functions deploy <fn> --project-ref aigchluqluzuhtbfllgh` bundles and pushes from disk in one command — no context hop, no retyping, and `scripts/deploy-edge.sh` stays useful for the local verify step. The old `nyx-cli-deploy` PAT is already flagged for revocation, so this is a fresh token, not a reinstatement. Keep the MCP path documented as the fallback. | Now | 2026-07-26 | B-351 slice 6 deploy (blocked on it today); every future Edge-Function deploy | Open — PM action (mint + set the secret) |
| B-472 | `delete-account` still passes vet-attachment paths to the service-role purge unscoped | Filed by the `rls-privacy-reviewer` pass on migration 043 (B-248/B-466), deliberately out of that schema PR's scope — an Edge Function change does not belong in a migration PR (the same split migration 042 made). 043's CHECK (`starts_with(storage_path, pet_id::text || '/')`) makes the crafted cross-tenant path unrepresentable, which is the real fix and is why this is defense-in-depth rather than a live hole. The precise residual: `starts_with` is a PREFIX test, so `{ownPetId}/../{victimPetId}/x.jpg` **passes** the CHECK *and* the storage INSERT policy (its first folder segment is legitimately owned), and reaches `adminClient.storage.remove()` verbatim because `vetAttachmentPaths` gets `cleanPaths` only (`plan.ts:215`), which never normalises a path. It deletes nothing — `storage.objects.name` is an opaque literal and neither storage-api nor S3 resolves `..` — so the boundary HOLDS, but it holds on a third-party implementation detail we neither own nor test. Fix: a `scopeVetAttachmentPaths` mirroring `scopeFoodPaths` (`plan.ts:203-208`) — **exact first-segment set membership, never `startsWith`**, which is precisely why `scopeFoodPaths` was written that way. Note the identical prefix-CHECK shape exists in **025** for `event_attachments`, whose downstream service-role reader is `generate-report` rather than the purge — trace that one separately when building this; do not assume it behaves the same. | Later | 2026-07-26 | — (defense-in-depth; the binding CHECK shipped in 043) | Open |
| B-491 | The simple/symptom + weight confirm buttons have the same double-submit hole B-336 just closed on the pickers | _(Filed as B-477 on 2026-07-26; **renumbered to B-491** the same day — B-477 was taken on `main` by the raw-provider-string-leak row from #470, and the first renumber, B-485, was then taken by the build-35 `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` row. Both caught by the new B-435 check.)_ Found while fixing B-336 (2026-07-26). The picker paths are now guarded, but the OTHER two write paths on `app/log.tsx` are still bare `TouchableOpacity`s over an async write with no in-flight state: the simple/symptom step's confirm button (`onPress={() => handleConfirm()}`, two call sites) and the weight step's (`onPress={handleConfirmWeight}`). A rapid double-tap on either writes two events — the same bug, a colder path (these steps have a deliberate confirm action rather than a one-tap tile, so the taps are less bursty, and a duplicate vomit/weight row is less clinically loaded than a duplicate dose). Deliberately left out of B-336's scope, which was the picker press path. The fix is now a drop-in: `useSubmitGuard` already exists, so it's a `guardSubmit(() => …)` wrap plus making `handleConfirm`/`handleConfirmWeight` return whether they committed (`handleConfirm` already returns a nullable result; `handleConfirmWeight` returns void and needs the boolean threaded through its early returns). | Later | 2026-07-26 | Extends B-336; same file (`app/log.tsx`), same hook | Open |
| B-492 | Seven duplicate backlog IDs — B-432, B-441, B-442, B-443, B-463, B-464, B-465 each name two unrelated items | **Found while closing B-455/B-442 (B-417 PR 7), then demonstrated twice more by the row you are reading.** Parallel sessions mint the same next-ID against the same file, so `grep -o '^| B-[0-9]*' docs/backlog.md \| sort \| uniq -d` returns seven collisions. It is not cosmetic: a row is the unit of reference here — `lib/dietTrialDayMath.guard.test.ts` cited &ldquo;B-442&rdquo; in a comment and a reader has no way to tell which of the two rows it meant, and a session closing one silently appears to close the other. **This row is its own third and fourth data point.** It was filed as B-472–B-476, renumbered to B-477–B-481 when #468 landed IDs it had already taken, and renumbered again to B-482 when #470 published a *different* B-477 — i.e. the file collided twice during the single session that was filing the collision report, and the second collision was on the report's own ID. (Both were caught before merge and resolved by renumbering the unpublished side, which is why they are not in the seven above; the seven are the ones nobody caught in time. That is the real measure — two of this session's four collisions were caught only because someone happened to re-grep.) Fix is a renumbering sweep that keeps the OLDER row's ID and reassigns the newer, plus a `grep`-based uniqueness check the `backlog-groomer` skill runs. The allocator is the root cause and no convention fixes it: `Added` dates are identical, because every session reads the file before any of them writes. | Next | 2026-07-26 | Product Owner / `backlog-groomer` | Open |
| B-493 | The vet report's diet-trial block has no `pm-feature-review`/Designer pass, and its copy is un-workshopped | **Recorded at B-417 PR 7, not a defect.** The block was written against §7 + the LOCKED §5.2 strings and cold-read by `vet-report-cold-read`, which is the right reviewer for a clinician-facing artifact — but the §7 mock round (C4, deferred to it) rendered the trial block in ONLY the two variants the PM chose between, and several strings here have no mock at all: the antigen-tally line, the &ldquo;Also during the trial&rdquo; group, the four §7.2 caveat clauses, and the medication-overlap framing. None is owner-facing, so `nyx-voice` is not the gate; what is missing is a Designer read of the block's HIERARCHY against Principle 6 (it is now the longest single block on page 1, above the symptom trend) and a Dr. Chen ratification of the two-element scope as SHIPPED rather than as specified. | Next | 2026-07-26 | Step 9 polish | Open |
| B-494 | A cat refusing nearly every meal of a prescribed diet raises NO safety flag on the vet report | **Found by the `vet-report-cold-read` on B-417 PR 7, and it is the most serious finding of that pass.** The rendered artifact holds an 8-year-old cat with **34 of 38 feedings of the trial diet logged as refused across 19 days**, **&minus;0.3 kg on a 4.4 kg body (~6.8% of body weight in 18 days)**, active chronic vomiting, and a free-fed bowl whose intake is by definition unobserved — the canonical feline anorexia / hepatic-lipidosis picture — and `snapshot.safetyFlags` is **empty**, so the report renders **no safety band at all**. The quieter-looking of the two artifacts is the sicker patient. Root cause is known and documented, not mysterious: `detectIntakeDecline` is a RELATIVE-decline detector, so a diet refused from day 1 is uniformly low rather than declining and returns `{status:'none'}` — PR 5's `trialDietRefusal` exists precisely because of this and is rendered on the card and in the trial block, but it is **not a `SafetyFlag`**, so it never reaches the band. Fix direction: either a trial-refusal safety lane keyed on PR 5's already-tuned floors (`REFUSAL_MIN_RATED`/`MIN_DAYS`/`SHARE`), or extend the intake detector to fire on a sustained ABSOLUTE floor rather than a relative fall. **Deliberately not done in PR 7** — a new clinical escalation path is load-bearing and needs the `adversarial-reviewer` pass + Dr. Chen that the DoD requires, and PR 7's mandated gate was the cold read. `clinical-guardrails` applies; intake is not preference. **Two cold reads disagree on whether this blocks the report, and the disagreement is not resolved:** round 3's Dr. Chen **withdrew it as a blocker** once page 1 composed the refusal with the weight delta as % of body weight; round 4's Dr. Chen — a fresh context, given the same artifact — ranked it **blocking #1**, on the ground that the report *teaches* the reader to scan the flag zone (*"Safety flags — Shown only when present, above the fold"*) and the honest legend disclaiming it sits on the last page, so composition mid-page does not repair the 60-second scan. Their words: *"the quieter-looking of the two artifacts is the sicker patient."* **RULED 2026-07-26 — the PM delegated the call to the product team, and the team ruled it BLOCKING FOR THE DEPLOY, not for the merge.** Rationale, and the conflict resolved rather than split: Dr. Chen carries this lens and two of three independent cold reads called it blocking, with a mechanism neither the Designer's nor Jordan's counter-lens rebuts — the report *teaches* the reader to scan the flag zone, and the legend then states affirmatively that no reduced-intake flag fired, so an empty band is read as a negative result rather than as silence. That is reassurance-on-absence at the report layer, which `clinical-guardrails` forbids outright, and *intake is not preference* routes refusal toward a health flag by invariant. The usual counter-argument — alarm fatigue, don't cry wolf — does not apply here: 38-of-38 refusals with ~7% body-weight loss in an 8-year-old cat with active chronic vomiting is not a marginal detector firing, it is the canonical case. **So: B-417 PR 7 merges (its own trial block is correct and regression-tested), `generate-report` is NOT redeployed until this lane exists,** and the artifact an owner can generate keeps the pre-PR-7 behaviour in the meantime. B-479 is therefore the gate on the redeploy, and it still needs its own `adversarial-reviewer` pass + Dr. Chen sign-off per the DoD — this ruling sets the bar, it does not waive the gates. | Now | 2026-07-26 | **Gates the `generate-report` redeploy** (B-417 PR 7 is merged but deliberately not deployed) | Open — ruled blocking for deploy 2026-07-26; build next. |
| B-495 | The vet report's weight tile is species-blind and states no % of body weight | **Found by the `vet-report-cold-read` on B-417 PR 7** (pre-existing, not introduced by it). A 32 kg Labrador and a 4.4 kg cat get the identical rendering: `-0.3 kg · home-scale trajectory (descriptive)`, greyed, in the fourth tile. On the cat that is **~6.8% of body mass** and, in a refusing vomiting animal, the single most action-forcing number on the page; no percentage appears anywhere in the report. The `(descriptive)` label is right about provenance and wrong about consequence. Fix direction: render the delta as a percentage alongside the absolute, computed against the earliest in-window weigh-in, and let the species set what magnitude reads as notable — without inventing a clinical threshold (which is a Dr. Chen call, not an arithmetic one). Note the honest constraint: these are owner home-scale readings, so the percentage inherits their noise, and a percentage stated more precisely than the instrument supports is its own defect. | Next | 2026-07-26 | Step 9 polish; independent of B-417 | Open |
| B-496 | The symptom chart's intervention marker is WEEK-granular and centred, so it does not point at the day the caption promises | **Found by the `vet-report-cold-read` on B-417 PR 7 round 3** (pre-existing chart behaviour, not introduced by it). The caption reads *&ldquo;A dashed vertical marks when a diet, medication, or supplement **started**&rdquo;*, but `symptomChart` draws the line at `centerX(bucketIndex)` — the **centre of the 7-day bucket** the start falls in — so a trial starting on the window's first day draws its line ~3.5 days in, and two starts in the same week collapse to one marker (`markedBuckets` de-dupes by bucket, deliberately, to avoid overlapping verticals). The reviewer inferred a mis-render from two reports both marking `x=82.0`; that part is a false alarm — both trials start in bucket 0 and the chart geometry is identical, so the coordinate is correct **given** week granularity. The real defect is the caption over-promising day precision the mark does not have, and the collapse hiding a second start. Cheapest honest fix is the caption (*&ldquo;marks the week an intervention started&rdquo;*) plus a count when a bucket carries more than one; the better fix is day-granular x with a de-collide rule, which changes chart geometry and therefore wants its own `vet-report-cold-read`. Verify against a rendered PNG, not SVG coordinates. | Next | 2026-07-26 | Step 9 polish | Open |
| B-497 | The symptom charts plot a labelled `0` for weeks in which NOTHING was logged, and the `aria-label` asserts it | **Found by `vet-report-cold-read` round 4 on B-417 PR 7** (pre-existing chart behaviour). On the refused-cat artifact the vomiting chart emits zero-nubs with `&lt;text class="z num"&gt;0&lt;/text&gt;` for the two buckets after logging stopped, and the accessible name reads `aria-label="Vomiting episodes per week: 2, 2, 1, 0, 0."` — so the most reassuring object on the page is a resolution curve whose last 40% is **absence of a log rendered as a measured zero**, on a page that elsewhere says *"Nothing was logged on 13 of 32 days."* This is reassurance-on-absence in the one place a vet reads fastest, and the screen-reader path carries the claim with **no caveat at all**. Compounding: the off-diet chart draws **nothing** for a zero week while the symptom charts draw a labelled nub, so on the one chart where blank means "clean week" blank is indistinguishable from "no data" — three charts on one page with two different meanings for empty. Fix direction: a distinct no-data rendering (hatch or gap, never a `0`) for buckets with no log of any kind, applied consistently across all three charts, with the `aria-label` naming those buckets as unlogged. Wants its own cold read on a rendered PNG, since it is chart geometry. `clinical-guardrails`: n=1 never reassures, and absence is not wellness. | Now | 2026-07-26 | Step 9 polish; independent of B-417 | Open |
| B-498 | Vet-report chart y-axis: the mid gridline is mislabelled on odd maxima | **Found by `vet-report-cold-read` round 4 on B-417 PR 7** (pre-existing geometry). On the dog artifact's itching chart the axis places `0` at y=116 and `5` at y=28 — 17.6 px per unit — while the gridline **labelled `3`** is drawn at y=72, i.e. the value **2.5**. A bar of 3 therefore tops out visibly *above* its own labelled gridline. Even maxima scale correctly, so the defect fires only on odd ones — and it fired on the headline clinical trend. Value labels on the bars limit the damage, but any vet reading bar heights against gridlines misreads every bar. Verify on a rendered PNG. | Next | 2026-07-26 | Step 9 polish | Open |

The single partial meal has no date, no food and no rating anywhere in the document. By the report's own invariant (*"a declined or barely-touched meal is a possible health signal, never 'picky'"* — intake is not preference) that one datum is precisely the one that must be traceable, and the grouping hides it while the caption promises otherwise.
