Distinct from B-479 (no safety flag fires at all) — this is a factual gap on a surface that already renders, and it is the report's own honest-silence principle failing to state its silence. Note the invariant it must respect: this is a statement about the RECORD, never a reassurance or an alarm derived from absence. | Now | 2026-07-26 | Step 9; read alongside B-479 | Open |
| B-508 | Diet trial: "Stopped early" is a category error at day 56, and the completed path captures no reason | **Found by `pm-feature-review` (Jordan) + `adversarial-reviewer` on B-417 PR 6.** An owner who finished all 56 days and whose vet said switch taps the only button that records *why* and is asked **"What got in the way?"** over copy reading *"Trials get stopped early all the time"* — nothing got in the way; she completed the window. The reason taxonomy is shaped for a day-19 abandonment and is wired to the end-of-window moment. Conversely `This trial is done` writes `stopped_reason = null`, so a vet gets a verdict with no why and *"we finished the window and the vet said switch"* is unrecordable except by mislabelling it as stopping early. Options: collapse the end-of-window moment into one path collecting BOTH a reason and an outcome, or add a completed-path reason set. Changes the design lock, so it is a PM call. | Next | 2026-07-26 | B-417 follow-up | Open — PM decision (surfaced 2026-07-26, PR #481) |
| B-509 | Diet trial: `Save` ends a medical intervention with no confirm, no undo and no way back | **Found by `pm-feature-review` on B-417 PR 6.** The outcome sheet is titled *"What changed over the 8 weeks"* and its button says **Save**; pressing it writes `status='completed'` and the completed card’s only action is "Open vet report". Jordan reading this thinks she is saving a note. Meanwhile the *reversible* action (`Keep going`) carries the visual weight and the *irreversible* one carries the softer verb — the escape hierarchy is inverted. Related: there is no edit-outcome, no reopen and no undo, so a mis-tap on day 56 (or a "Better" reconsidered after the recheck) is permanent in the record the vet reads. Candidate fix: `End the trial` + a one-line consequence under it, plus an edit path from the terminal card. | Next | 2026-07-26 | B-417 follow-up | Open — PM decision (surfaced 2026-07-26, PR #481) |
| B-510 | Diet trial: the GI indication gets the MOST invitations to stop and the smallest extension | **Found by `adversarial-reviewer` on B-417 PR 6 — a ratification question, not a bug.** `extensionDays` gives GI +14d and skin +28d against defaults of 28d and 56d, so a dog·gut trial re-presents `This trial is done` at days 28, 42, 56, 70, 84 — **five** exposures of the stop button before ACVIM’s ≥12 weeks; a skin trial gets **two**. The indication carrying the live clinical harm therefore gets the most prompts to stop AND the smaller one-tap extension. §4.3 specifies both numbers so the code is spec-conformant, but the module’s claim that +14d "carries the assessment window toward ACVIM’s ≥12-week continuation" is not what the arithmetic does. Dr. Chen ratification; pairs with P-1. | Next | 2026-07-26 | Dr. Chen ratification (with P-1) | Open — surfaced 2026-07-26 (PR #481) |
| B-511 | Diet trial: a refused-diet trial is a dead end — the health lane it "routes to" never fires | **Found by `adversarial-reviewer` on B-417 PR 6.** §4.3 says a refusal `stopped_reason` routes to the intake-decline health lane. PR 6 implements that as the copy register plus a structural refusal to score (no compliance outcome can attach) — and says so plainly — but no detector fires. The gap that makes it matter: `detectIntakeDecline` needs **rated meals**, and an owner who stops logging a bowl the cat won’t touch produces none. So the single place the app is told *"this cat refused the prescribed diet for three weeks"* is a terminal screen, and it says less than the app says about one skipped dinner. Same neighbourhood as B-494 (a refusing patient raising no safety flag). A new escalation path needs its own `adversarial-reviewer` pass + Dr. Chen. | Next | 2026-07-26 | B-494 / the refusal safety lane | Open — surfaced 2026-07-26 (PR #481) |
| B-512 | Diet trial: the completion milestone is invisible off the Pet tab | **Found by `pm-feature-review` on B-417 PR 6.** On day 56 the Home strip reads *"Diet trial · day 56 of 56"* with a full bar — structurally identical to day 30 — and the widget header the same. `TrialStrip.tsx` says out loud why the strip exists (*"the Pet tab is not a surface the wedge owner visits daily"*), and then the one moment the feature was built for adds nothing to it. The milestone also visibly demotes after 24h: three buttons on day 56, a text link from day 57 (state 6), so an owner who doesn’t open the Pet tab on exactly day 56 never sees it. Both surfaces already render a day counter, so the cue is cheap. | Next | 2026-07-26 | B-417 follow-up | Open — surfaced 2026-07-26 (PR #481) |
| B-513 | Diet trial: a decline-flagged trial has no designed decision surface | **Found by `adversarial-reviewer` + `pm-feature-review` on B-417 PR 6, partially fixed in-PR.** The intake-decline branch returned `actions: []` unconditionally, so while the flag was live a trial that had reached its window could not be ended at all — §4.3’s "never expires and re-surfaces until acted on" silently failed and §7 rendered a stopped intervention as ongoing, on the sickest patient the feature has. PR 6 restored a single quiet link at/after target. What is still undesigned: whether a declining pet’s outcome sheet should accept `Better` at all (it currently can), and what the right register is for ending a trial on an animal that has stopped eating. Needs a mock round + Dr. Chen, not a build-time call. | Next | 2026-07-26 | B-474 mock round | Open — surfaced 2026-07-26 (PR #481) |
| B-514 | Three pre-existing test fixtures fail outside UTC, so CI cannot see them | **Found while fixing B-417 PR 6’s own timezone bug.** `lib/analytics.test.ts` (`getDietTrialProgress → day-counts inclusively`), `lib/widgetSnapshot.test.ts` (`buildWidgetSnapshot`) and `lib/widgetResolution.test.ts` (`resolveTrialContext`) fail under `TZ=Pacific/Auckland` / `TZ=Pacific/Kiritimati` and two under `TZ=Asia/Kolkata`; a fourth, `constants/monetizationCopy.test.ts` (`formatResetLabel → monthly`), fails under `TZ=America/Los_Angeles`. All green in UTC. These are **TZ-fragile fixtures, not production bugs** — B-421’s own explicit-zone tests all pass — but CI runs in UTC only, so the suite silently under-tests the exact axis B-421 exists to defend, and PR 6 shipped a real inversion that UTC could not see. Fix: pin the fixtures to explicit zones (as B-421’s do) and/or add a second CI matrix leg at a non-UTC offset. | Next | 2026-07-26 | CI hardening (B-390 follow-up) | Open — surfaced 2026-07-26 (PR #481) |
| B-515 | Diet trial: the outcome sheet’s symptom counts are not pseudoreplication-safe | **Named by `adversarial-reviewer` on B-417 PR 6 as NOT falsified — recorded so nobody logs it as reviewed.** The before/during counts are raw event counts with no episode collapse, so one symptom bout logged three times inflates whichever side it falls on. This is not a break of PR 6 (the spec does not ask for collapse, and `computeSymptomCounts` is deliberately raw app-wide so a count card matches the History timeline the owner can scroll), but the outcome sheet is the one surface where the two sides are *compared* rather than listed — which is where uneven logging granularity turns into an apparent effect. Needs a Data Scientist call on whether comparison surfaces want a different estimator from list surfaces. | Later | 2026-07-26 | B-417 follow-up | Open — surfaced 2026-07-26 (PR #481) |
| B-516 | Diet trial: the sparse-before disclosure leaks just above its threshold | **Found by the second `adversarial-reviewer` pass on B-417 PR 6.** `isSparseBefore` is `beforeLoggedDays * 2 < beforeDays` with no clustering or recency term, so **29 of 56** observable days all clustered in the 4 weeks immediately before the trial — the attention *and* symptom peak — renders the undisclosed `Compared with the 8 weeks before it started.` above `Itch/Scratch: 22 before · 3 during.` Same bias as the 4-of-56 case at half strength, one day above the trigger; exact half does not fire either. The count-based disclosure is a strict improvement on the all-or-nothing boolean it replaced and §5.2 explicitly leaves the floor to PR 5, so this is a residual rather than a break — but a clustering or recency term is what would actually close it. | Later | 2026-07-26 | B-474 / PR 5 floor work | Open — surfaced 2026-07-26 (PR #481) |
| B-517 | The day-math guard does not cover the fourth consumer it was written to catch | **Found by the second `adversarial-reviewer` pass on B-417 PR 6.** `lib/dietTrialDayMath.guard.test.ts` enforces one day-math oracle via a `CONSUMERS` list plus a `DAY_DIVISION` regex. `lib/dietTrialOutcomeFacts.ts` declares its own `MS_PER_DAY` and its own `todayIndex - startIndex + 1`, is **not** in `CONSUMERS`, and its `index * MS_PER_DAY` evades the regex (which matches division only). PR 6 named this file as B-421’s fourth path and then pinned it with value tests, which is why its inversion was caught by review rather than by the guard — and nothing stops a fifth. Fix: add the file to `CONSUMERS` and widen the regex to multiplication, or hoist the epoch-day helpers into `lib/utils` so there is one implementation to guard. | Next | 2026-07-26 | B-421 follow-up | Open — surfaced 2026-07-26 (PR #481) |
| B-518 | Diet trial: two similar-sounding day ratios with different numerators on one sheet | **Found by the second `adversarial-reviewer` pass on B-417 PR 6.** The outcome sheet renders *"only 4 of those 56 days have anything logged"* (any-event numerator) two lines above *"Days you logged any food: 1 of 56 before"* (meal-day numerator). Not false — meal days are a subset of any-event days — but it is the same collision class PR 6 just fixed between the card’s coverage line and the sheet’s density line, where two metrics shared a name. Either name the first one’s numerator explicitly or derive both from one series. | Later | 2026-07-26 | B-417 follow-up | Open — surfaced 2026-07-26 (PR #481) |
| B-519 | Sign-out file wipe is a hardcoded UNION and fails open — derive it like B-424 did for rows | `clearLocalData` (`lib/db.ts`) deletes captured local files by unioning `local_uri` from a HANDCODED list of tables. A mirror table missing from it has its rows wiped by `LOCAL_WIPE_TABLES` while its **files stay on disk** — and once the row is gone nothing can ever find them again. Exactly the fail-open shape B-424 eliminated for the ROW half (`hydration.test.ts` derives the wipe set from a real `sqlite_master`, so a new table breaks the build); the FILE half has no equivalent guard. Found by the B-478 VF-1 `rls-privacy-reviewer`, which caught `vet_documents` missing from the UNION — fixed in that PR, but the *guard* is still absent, so the next table repeats it. Fix: derive the file-bearing table set from the schema constants (any table with a `local_uri` column) and assert the UNION covers it. | Next | 2026-07-26 | Any new local table carrying a `local_uri` | Open |
| B-520 | `enforce_dose_paired_event_same_pet` (023) + `enforce_diet_trial_food_same_pet` (041) are RLS-blind — make them SECURITY DEFINER | Both are SECURITY INVOKER, so their cross-table lookups are RLS-filtered and cannot see another account's row. Neither is exposed **today** because both are `NOT EXISTS(match) → RAISE`, i.e. the fails-CLOSED polarity — but that is an accident of polarity, not a design property, and inverting either predicate would silently flip it to fail-open. B-478 VF-1's `rls-privacy-reviewer` demonstrated exactly that flip on `vet_documents`' group check (an `EXISTS → RAISE` check, which allowed a cross-account `document_group_id` collision); migration 045 fixed that one with SECURITY DEFINER + a pinned `search_path`. These two are the same class on tables with LIVE rows, so they were deliberately left out of that boundary hotfix. Also consider the advisory-lock TOCTOU fix 045 applied. | Next | 2026-07-26 | — | Open |
| B-521 | Tighten `vet_documents_storage_path_pet_prefix` from a prefix test to a full-shape regex | `starts_with(storage_path, pet_id || '/')` admits `{ownPetId}/../{victimPetId}/x.pdf` — the first FOLDER segment is a pet the caller owns, so it also passes the `nyx-vet-documents` Storage INSERT policy. Harmless today (Storage treats `objects.name` as an opaque literal, so the key matches no object) and `scopeVetDocumentPaths` drops it before the service-role purge — but a CHECK binds the **service role** too, whereas the scope function guards one caller. A regex pinning the whole `{pet_id}/{uuid}.{ext}` shape would make the crafted row unrepresentable. Deferred from B-478 VF-1: migration 044 is applied, and tightening an existing CHECK is a different risk profile from adding one. | Later | 2026-07-26 | Before VF-3 puts real objects in the bucket | Open |
